`README.md`:

```md
<p align="center">
  <img src="assets/logo.png" alt="APK Analysis Toolkit" width="420">
</p>

<h1 align="center">APK Analysis Toolkit</h1>

<p align="center">
  A Rust terminal UI that bootstraps a rooted Android sandbox, bypasses SSL pinning,
  intercepts traffic through mitmproxy, and automatically flags malware-style red flags
  in any package — instead of hand-assembling Android Studio, Frida, mitmproxy, apktool,
  and JADX from a dozen separate guides.
</p>

<p align="center">
  <a href="https://0xramm.github.io/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/"><b>Documentation</b></a>
  ·
  <a href="https://github.com/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/releases/latest"><b>Latest Release</b></a>
</p>

<p align="center">
  <a href="https://github.com/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/releases/latest">
    <img src="https://img.shields.io/github/v/release/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit?label=latest%20release&color=blue" alt="Latest release">
  </a>
</p>

---

## What it does

One TUI, three menus:

**App manager (adb)** — list every installed package (no name-based filtering — type-to-filter search instead), pull detailed package info with automated red-flag detection (name impersonation, dangerous permission combos, signing certificate), install/uninstall/enable/disable/launch.

**Static Analysis Setup** — everything else that inspects an APK/package without needing it running:
- **APK tool (apktool)** — decompile a picked package's resources/manifest, or recompile an edited folder.
- **JADX** — decompile a picked package straight into `jadx-gui`.
- **MT Manager** — get an on-device file/APK manager installed on the emulator.

**Dynamic Analysis Setup** — everything that needs a running, rooted device:
- **Emulator** — detect/list AVDs (tagged rootable or not), create a rootable one, launch it, root/de-root.
- **SSL pinning bypass** — push `frida-server`, bypass pinning for a picked package via Frida's codeshare, track and reverse active sessions.
- **mitmproxy** — install, start the proxy + web UI (with a working auth token — no 403s), install its CA as a trusted system root (including the dm-verity reboot cycle most guides skip).
- **Run full setup (template)** — chains the whole dynamic pipeline end-to-end with live progress.

Every long-running step (system image downloads, pip installs, `frida-server`/apktool/JADX downloads) streams real progress instead of going silent, and the app checks GitHub for a newer release on every launch.

Full walkthrough, every gotcha it had to work around, and why: **[read the docs](https://0xramm.github.io/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/)**.

## Tools it wraps

This toolkit doesn't reimplement any of these — it detects, installs, configures, and drives them:

| Tool | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **Android SDK** (`adb`, `emulator`, `avdmanager`, `sdkmanager`, `aapt`, `apksigner`) | Emulator lifecycle, package inspection, manifest/signature checks |
| **Frida** (`frida-tools`, `frida-server`) + **objection** | Dynamic instrumentation and SSL-pinning bypass |
| **mitmproxy** (`mitmweb`) | HTTPS interception |
| **apktool** | APK resource/manifest decompilation and recompilation |
| **JADX** | Java/Kotlin source decompilation |
| **MT Manager** | On-device file/APK manager |
| `curl`, `openssl`, `xz`, `tar` | Downloads, certificate hashing, archive extraction — auto-discovered even when not on a shell's `PATH` |

## Install

Grab the latest `apkat-setup.exe` from **[Releases](https://github.com/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/releases/latest)** and run it — it installs `apkat.exe` and adds it to your `PATH`, so `apkat` works from any terminal. From then on it checks for and installs updates on its own.

Or build from source:

```bash
git clone https://github.com/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit.git
cd APK-Analysis-Toolkit
cargo build --release
```

See **[Installation](https://0xramm.github.io/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/getting-started/installation/)** for prerequisites (Android SDK, Python/pip, Java).

## Safety note

This kit is meant for **authorized pentesting, malware research, and CTFs** on devices/emulators you control. Always analyze untrusted APKs inside the emulator — ideally snapshotted and network-isolated from anything sensitive — never on a personal device.

## License

MIT.

# Starlight Starter Kit: Basics

[![Built with Starlight](https://astro.badg.es/v2/built-with-starlight/tiny.svg)](https://starlight.astro.build)

```
npm create astro@latest -- --template starlight
```

> 🧑‍🚀 **Seasoned astronaut?** Delete this file. Have fun!

## 🚀 Project Structure

Inside of your Astro + Starlight project, you'll see the following folders and files:

```
.
├── public/
├── src/
│   ├── assets/
│   ├── content/
│   │   └── docs/
│   └── content.config.ts
├── astro.config.mjs
├── package.json
└── tsconfig.json
```

Starlight looks for `.md` or `.mdx` files in the `src/content/docs/` directory. Each file is exposed as a route based on its file name.

Images can be added to `src/assets/` and embedded in Markdown with a relative link.

Static assets, like favicons, can be placed in the `public/` directory.

## 🧞 Commands

All commands are run from the root of the project, from a terminal:

| Command                   | Action                                           |
| :------------------------ | :----------------------------------------------- |
| `npm install`             | Installs dependencies                            |
| `npm run dev`             | Starts local dev server at `localhost:4321`      |
| `npm run build`           | Build your production site to `./dist/`          |
| `npm run preview`         | Preview your build locally, before deploying     |
| `npm run astro ...`       | Run CLI commands like `astro add`, `astro check` |
| `npm run astro -- --help` | Get help using the Astro CLI                     |

## 👀 Want to learn more?

Check out [Starlight’s docs](https://starlight.astro.build/), read [the Astro documentation](https://docs.astro.build), or jump into the [Astro Discord server](https://astro.build/chat).

```

// @ts-check
import { defineConfig } from 'astro/config';
import starlight from '@astrojs/starlight';

// https://astro.build/config
export default defineConfig({
	site: 'https://0xramm.github.io',
	base: '/APK-Analysis-Toolkit',
	integrations: [
		starlight({
			title: 'APK Analysis Toolkit',
			description:
				'A Rust TUI that bootstraps a rooted Android sandbox, SSL-pinning bypass, mitmproxy interception, and automated malware-style package analysis.',
			social: [{ icon: 'github', label: 'GitHub', href: 'https://github.com/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit' }],
			sidebar: [
				{
					label: 'Getting Started',
					items: [
						{ label: 'Overview', slug: 'getting-started/overview' },
						{ label: 'Installation', slug: 'getting-started/installation' },
						{ label: 'Quick Start', slug: 'getting-started/quick-start' },
					],
				},
				{
					label: 'App Manager',
					items: [
						{ label: 'Listing & Searching Packages', slug: 'app-manager/listing-and-filtering' },
						{ label: 'Package Info & Red Flags', slug: 'app-manager/package-info-and-red-flags' },
					],
				},
				{
					label: 'Static Analysis Setup',
					items: [
						{ label: 'APK Tool (apktool)', slug: 'static-analysis/apktool' },
						{ label: 'JADX', slug: 'static-analysis/jadx' },
						{ label: 'MT Manager', slug: 'static-analysis/mt-manager' },
					],
				},
				{
					label: 'Dynamic Analysis Setup',
					items: [
						{ label: 'Emulator: AVDs & Rooting', slug: 'emulator/avds-and-rooting' },
						{ label: 'SSL Pinning: Bypass & Re-pin', slug: 'ssl-pinning/bypass-and-repin' },
						{ label: 'mitmproxy: Proxy & CA Certificate', slug: 'mitmproxy/proxy-and-cert' },

				},
				{
					label: 'Operations',

						{ label: 'Multi-Device Selection', slug: 'operations/multi-device' },

					label: 'Troubleshooting',

				},
			],
		}),
	],
});

`docs/src/content/docs/app-manager/listing-and-filtering.md`:

```md
---
title: Listing & Searching Packages
description: Listing every installed package and narrowing the list with type-to-filter search.
---

## List installed packages

Runs `pm list packages` and prints the full, sorted result — every package, with no name-based hiding. On a stock emulator image that's 150+ entries including the `com.android.*`/`com.google.*` platform packages:

```
[OK] 206 packages
    android
    android.auto_generated_rro_product__
    android.auto_generated_rro_vendor__
    com.android.vending
    com.breel.wallpapers18
    com.google.android.gms
    com.google.android.gmscore
    ...
```

Nothing is filtered out by name. A malicious package can deliberately claim a `com.google.*`/`com.android.*`-style name specifically to hide in platform noise — filtering by name would reward exactly that trick, so the toolkit never does it. See [Package Info & Red Flags](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/app-manager/package-info-and-red-flags/) for how it catches disguised packages instead.

## Type-to-filter search

Every screen that shows a package list — Launch, Package Info, Install/Uninstall/Enable/Disable, and the SSL-pinning bypass picker — is the same picker widget with a live search box built in. Start typing and the list narrows to packages whose name contains what you've typed (case-insensitive substring match); `Backspace` removes characters, arrow keys move within the narrowed results, and `Enter` selects.

This replaces the need for any list-level filtering: instead of hiding packages ahead of time, you search down to the one you want on the fly, without ever losing visibility into the full list.

```

`docs/src/content/docs/app-manager/package-info-and-red-flags.md`:

```md
---
title: Package Info & Red Flags
description: Automated malware-style triage for any installed package.
---

`Package info` pulls together everything extractable about a package from the device and the APK itself, and — unlike a plain `dumpsys` dump — automatically flags what's actually suspicious, right at the top of the report.

## What it gathers

1. **`dumpsys package <pkg>`** — identity (version, install/update times, installer), install paths, flags, requested/runtime permissions. The full raw output is appended at the end so nothing is left out even if a future Android version changes field names the curated sections look for.
2. **The APK itself**, pulled once via `adb pull` and reused for both of the next two checks:
   - **`aapt dump badging`** — the app's real **label** (what you actually see on the device, as opposed to its package ID), its launchable activity class name (handy if you want to Frida-hook the entry point directly), and manifest-declared SDK versions.
   - **`apksigner verify --print-certs`** — the real signing certificate (DN + SHA-256). A package name or permission list can lie; the signer is cryptographic.

Package name and app label are shown immediately, before anything else — a mismatch between the two (package claims to be a system component, label says something else entirely) is exactly the kind of thing worth seeing without scrolling.

## Automated red flags

The report opens with a `!! RED FLAGS !!` section (if any fire) before the routine detail sections:

| Check | Fires when |
|---|---|
| Name/location impersonation | Package name starts with `com.google.*`/`com.android.*`/`android.*`, but `codePath` is `/data/app/...` (sideloaded) instead of `/system`, `/product`, `/vendor`, or `/apex` |
| Placeholder version | `versionCode=1` and/or `versionName=1.0` |
| Debuggable | `DEBUGGABLE` flag set — unusual for a release build |
| Sensitive permissions | 14 tracked permissions, each with a plain-English reason — `INSTALL_PACKAGES`/`REQUEST_DELETE_PACKAGES`/`OVERRIDE_PACKAGE_VERIFICATION` (dropper capability), `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES`, `BIND_ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICE` (banking-trojan overlay technique), `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW`, `BIND_DEVICE_ADMIN`, SMS/contacts/call-log/camera/microphone access |
| AOSP test-key signature | Signing DN matches AOSP's publicly known `testkey` (`build/target/product/security/testkey.*` in the AOSP source) — legitimate Google/OEM production apps are **never** signed with it |

## Worked example

During development, this exact flow caught a real sample: an APK downloaded as a fake Indian vehicle-registration app ("mParivahan") whose manifest package name was `com.google.android.gmscore` — deliberately impersonating Google Play Services' package ID so it would sit unnoticed among 200+ platform packages in a plain list (exactly why the package list is never filtered by name — see [Listing & Searching Packages](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/app-manager/listing-and-filtering/)).

```
Package: com.google.android.gmscore
App label: NxtGen mParivahan

-- !! RED FLAGS (9) !! --
  ! Package name 'com.google.android.gmscore' impersonates a system/Google component
    (app label: "NxtGen mParivahan"), but is installed as a regular sideloaded app
    (codePath=/data/app/...). Real system packages live in /system, /product, or
    /vendor — never /data/app.
  ! Placeholder version (versionCode=1 / versionName=1.0) — unusual for any app
    with real update history.
  ! Marked DEBUGGABLE — unusual for a release build; expands attack surface.
  ! Requests INSTALL_PACKAGES — can silently install other apps (dropper capability).
  ! Requests REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES — can request installing other apps.
  ! Requests REQUEST_DELETE_PACKAGES — can request removing other apps.
  ! Requests OVERRIDE_PACKAGE_VERIFICATION — can bypass Play Protect / package verification.
  ! Requests QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES — can enumerate every installed app.
  ! Signed with AOSP's publicly known "testkey" — legitimate Google/OEM production
    apps are NEVER signed with this key. Near-definitive evidence of a repackaged
    or tampered APK.
```

Real Google Play Services is a system component baked into `/system`, is never a standalone downloadable `.apk`, and is never signed with AOSP's public test key. The permission combination (`INSTALL_PACKAGES` + `OVERRIDE_PACKAGE_VERIFICATION` + `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES`) is a classic **dropper/loader** profile — its job is most likely installing a second-stage payload while wearing a trusted name, counting on neither the user nor casual tooling to look closely.

```

`docs/src/content/docs/emulator/avds-and-rooting.md`:

```md
---
title: AVDs & Rooting
description: Detecting, creating, launching, rooting, and de-rooting Android virtual devices.
---

## Why rootability matters

Android system images come in two flavors:

- **Play Store / production images** — locked down like a retail phone. `adb root` is disabled unconditionally; there is no workaround. Any package with `PlayStore.enabled=true` in its AVD `config.ini` falls in this category.
- **Google APIs (no Play Store) images** — development builds where `adb root` works natively.

Anything requiring root — pushing `frida-server` so it can actually spawn/attach to processes, writing the mitmproxy CA into `/system`— only works on the second kind.

## Detect & list AVDs

Lists every AVD `emulator -list-avds` reports, with a condensed summary per entry (Android version, ABI, RAM) and a rootability tag:

```
[OK] AVDs found:
    - pentest-avd  (Android 33, x86_64, 2G RAM, rootable)
    - Medium_Phone  (Android 37.0, x86_64, 2816MB RAM, Play Store, no root)
```

Also reports how many devices are currently online, warning if more than one is attached (see [Multi-Device Selection](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/operations/multi-device/)).

## Create new AVD

Creates `pentest-avd` using `system-images;android-33;google_apis;x86_64` — Google APIs, no Play Store, x86_64 — the combination that's both rootable and broadly compatible. The system-image download streams live percentage progress the whole way through (`Fetch remote repository... → Downloading ...zip... → Unzipping...`), since this can be a multi-minute, gigabyte-scale download.

Only runs if no rootable AVD already exists — the **Run full setup** pipeline checks this automatically rather than assuming the first AVD it finds is usable.

## Launch existing AVD

Boots whichever AVD is preferred (rootable one first, falling back to whatever exists), always with `-writable-system` so a later `remount` can succeed. Clears stale `adb` server state first, since ghost device entries can otherwise interfere with targeting.

## Root device / De-root device

Thin wrappers around `adb root` / `adb unroot`. On a Play Store image, `adb root` fails with `adbd cannot run as root in production builds` — the tool detects this exact message and tells you to use **Create new AVD** instead of just reporting a bare failure.

```
[FAIL] adb root: adbd cannot run as root in production builds
This device's image doesn't support root (it's a Play Store system image —
adb root is disabled unconditionally on these, no workaround exists).
Run 'Create new AVD': it builds a Google APIs image (no Play Store, Android 13,
x86_64) that's rootable and has the widest package/API compatibility for testing.
```

```

`docs/src/content/docs/getting-started/installation.md`:

```md
---
title: Installation
description: How to install the APK Analysis Toolkit and its prerequisites.
---

## Download a release

Grab the latest `apkat-setup.exe` from the [Releases page](https://github.com/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/releases) and run it. The installer copies `apkat.exe` into place and adds its install folder to your user `PATH`, so `apkat` works from any terminal afterward — no admin rights needed. Once installed, the binary checks for a newer release on every launch and updates itself automatically — see [Auto-Update & Releases](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/operations/auto-update/).

## Build from source

Requires a [Rust toolchain](https://rustup.rs/) (stable channel):

```bash
git clone https://github.com/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit.git
cd APK-Analysis-Toolkit
cargo build --release
```

The binary is produced at `target/release/apkat.exe`.

## Prerequisites

The toolkit shells out to external tools rather than reimplementing them — install whichever ones you plan to use:

| Tool | Used for | Auto-discovered from |
|---|---|---|
| Android Studio / SDK (`adb`, `emulator`, `avdmanager`, `sdkmanager`, `aapt`, `apksigner`) | Everything emulator- and package-related | `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Android\Sdk` (or `ANDROID_SDK_ROOT`/`ANDROID_HOME`) |
| Python + `pip` | Installing `frida-tools`, `objection`, `mitmproxy` | System `PATH` |
| `curl` | Downloading `frida-server`, checking for updates | System `PATH` (Windows 10 1803+ ships one natively) |
| `openssl` | Hashing the mitmproxy CA cert for Android's trust store | System `PATH`, or Git for Windows' bundled copy |
| `xz` | Decompressing downloaded `frida-server` builds | System `PATH`, or Git for Windows' bundled copy |

You don't need all of these up front — each module checks for what it needs and tells you exactly what's missing and how to get it, the first time you use it.

```

`docs/src/content/docs/getting-started/overview.md`:

```md
---
title: Overview
description: What the APK Analysis Toolkit is and who it's for.
---

The APK Analysis Toolkit is a single Rust terminal UI (TUI) that bootstraps a full Android pentest / malware-analysis sandbox, instead of hand-assembling Android Studio, Frida, objection, and mitmproxy from separate guides.

## Who it's for

Security researchers, malware analysts, and pentesters who need to:

- Stand up a rootable Android emulator quickly, without guessing which system image supports root.
- Bypass SSL/certificate pinning on a target app to inspect its traffic.
- Intercept and decrypt HTTPS traffic via mitmproxy, with the device's trust store set up correctly the first time.
- Triage a suspicious APK — package identity, permissions, signing certificate — without manually running `dumpsys`, `aapt`, and `apksigner` by hand every time.
- Decompile an APK's resources/manifest (apktool) or its Java source (JADX) without separately installing and wiring up either tool.

## How it's organized

The main menu splits into three branches of screens:

**App Manager (adb)** — list every installed package (no name-based filtering — [search instead](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/app-manager/listing-and-filtering/)), [detailed package info with automated red-flag detection](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/app-manager/package-info-and-red-flags/), install/uninstall/enable/disable/launch.

**Static Analysis Setup** — everything else that inspects an APK/package without needing it running:

- **[APK tool (apktool)](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/static-analysis/apktool/)** — decompile a picked package's resources/manifest, or recompile an edited folder.
- **[JADX](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/static-analysis/jadx/)** — decompile a picked package straight into `jadx-gui`.
- **[MT Manager](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/static-analysis/mt-manager/)** — get an on-device file/APK manager installed on the emulator.

**Dynamic Analysis Setup** — everything that needs a running, rooted device:

- **Emulator** — detect/list AVDs, create a rootable one, launch it, root/de-root.
- **SSL pinning bypass** — install Frida tooling, push `frida-server`, bypass/re-pin a picked package.
- **mitmproxy** — install, start the proxy + web UI, install the CA certificate as a trusted system root.
- **Run full setup (template)** — chains the whole dynamic-analysis pipeline end-to-end with live progress.

Plus, from the main menu directly: **Select ADB device** — pin a specific device when more than one is attached.

Every long-running step (system image downloads, pip installs, frida-server/apktool/JADX downloads) streams real progress instead of going silent, and the binary checks GitHub for a newer release on every launch.

## Design principles

- **No silent waits.** Anything that can take more than a few seconds streams live progress.
- **Detect, don't assume.** The tool actively checks whether an Android image is rootable, whether multiple devices are attached, and whether a package's identity matches reality — rather than assuming the happy path.
- **Document the gotchas.** Every Android/adb quirk this tool had to work around (see [Troubleshooting](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/troubleshooting/common-issues/)) is written down, not just silently patched.

```

`docs/src/content/docs/getting-started/quick-start.md`:

```md
---
title: Quick Start
description: Get a working rooted sandbox in one pass.
---

## Fastest path: Run full setup

From the main menu, select **Dynamic Analysis Setup → Run full setup (template)**. This single action:

1. Detects your Android SDK and lists existing AVDs, tagging each as rootable or not.
2. If no AVD exists — or none of the existing ones support root — creates a Google APIs (no Play Store) AVD, which is rootable and has broad package compatibility.
3. Launches it and waits for boot, with periodic progress (`still booting... (Ns)`).
4. Installs `frida-tools` and `objection` if missing.
5. Downloads, caches, and pushes a matching `frida-server` to the device.
6. Installs `mitmproxy` if missing, starts `mitmweb` (auto-opens your browser with a valid auth token), and points the device's system proxy at it.
7. Installs mitmproxy's CA certificate as a trusted system root — including the reboot cycle required on dm-verity–enabled images (see [Troubleshooting](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/troubleshooting/common-issues/)).

If any step fails, the pipeline **stops immediately** with a clear reason instead of continuing past a broken step — fix what it reports, then re-run.

## Navigating the TUI

| Key | Action |
|---|---|
| `↑`/`↓` or `j`/`k` | Move selection |
| `Enter` | Select / run |
| `Esc` or `Backspace` | Back to the previous menu |
| `PageUp` / `PageDown` | Scroll a long log/report |
| `c` | Copy the current log/report to the clipboard |
| `q` | Quit (from the main menu) |

Long-running steps show a spinner and live progress in the log pane — `Enter` is disabled until the current job finishes, but you can still browse with the arrow keys.

## Next steps

Once the pipeline completes, you have a rooted emulator with traffic routed through mitmproxy. From here:

- Install a target APK via **App Manager → Install APK**.
- Run **App Manager → Package info** on it first — the automated red-flags check (see [Package Info & Red Flags](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/app-manager/package-info-and-red-flags/)) is worth running on anything you didn't build yourself.
- Decompile it with **[APK tool](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/static-analysis/apktool/)** or **[JADX](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/static-analysis/jadx/)** if the red flags (or your own curiosity) warrant a closer look at what it actually does.
- Back in **Dynamic Analysis Setup → SSL pinning bypass → Bypass pinning for a package** if the app pins its own certificates.

```

<CardGrid stagger>

## Why this exists

Setting up an Android pentest/malware-analysis sandbox usually means stitching together Android Studio, Frida, objection, and mitmproxy by hand from a dozen different guides — and re-learning the same gotchas every time (dm-verity reboot cycles, ambiguous multi-device `adb` sessions, mitmweb's token requirement). This toolkit automates the whole pipeline in one terminal UI, and documents every gotcha it had to work around along the way.

`docs/src/content/docs/mitmproxy/proxy-and-cert.md`:

```md
---
title: Proxy & CA Certificate
description: Starting mitmweb, filtering noise, and getting its CA genuinely trusted on-device.
---

## Install mitmproxy

Installs via `pip` if `mitmweb` isn't already present, streaming progress live.

## Start proxy & point device at it

Starts `mitmweb` (not headless `mitmdump` — you get the live web UI) with two things wired in deliberately:

**A working auth token, and an actually-correct auto-open.** mitmweb normally generates its own random token and opens a browser tab to the right URL itself — but that only works if you let it manage the whole flow. This tool needs to *know* the token (to log the URL, and to be sure your browser is pointed at the right place), so it pins one itself via `--set web_password=<token>`, suppresses mitmweb's own browser launch (`--no-web-open-browser`, since with a fixed password its own URL omits the token and would 403), and opens the browser itself pointed at `http://127.0.0.1:8081/?token=<token>`.

**Noise filtering.** `--ignore-hosts` is passed for `connectivitycheck.gstatic.com`, `connectivitycheck.android.com`, and `play.googleapis.com` — a full TCP passthrough, so these never show up as flows at all (not just hidden by a UI filter).

Also sets the device's global HTTP proxy to `10.0.2.2:<port>` (the host, from the emulator's point of view).

## Install CA cert as system root

This is the step most guides gloss over, and the one most likely to silently fail. The full sequence:

1. Hashes mitmproxy's CA cert the way Android's system trust store expects (`openssl x509 ... -subject_hash_old`).
2. `adb root`, then **waits for the device to actually reconnect** before proceeding — `adb root` restarts `adbd`, causing a disconnect/reconnect window; issuing another command too soon can race it.
3. `adb remount`. On a **dm-verity-enabled image**, this doesn't make `/system` writable yet — it just disables verity and prints `Now reboot your device for settings to take effect`. The tool detects this exact message and:
   - Reboots the device and waits for it to finish booting.
   - Roots and remounts again — this time it actually succeeds (`remount succeeded`).
4. Pushes the cert to `/system/etc/security/cacerts/<hash>.0`, sets permissions, and reboots once more to load it.

The whole sequence pins one device serial for its duration (captured before the first `adb root`), so a mid-sequence `adb devices` hiccup during the reconnect can't make a later step land on a different device than the one that was just rooted.

:::note[Why Chrome might show "No internet"]
If the proxy is set but the CA isn't yet trusted, HTTPS connectivity checks (which Android runs over HTTPS) fail outright — Chrome reports this as "no internet," while plain HTTP content can still partially load. This is a strong signal the cert-install step hasn't completed successfully yet. See [Common Issues](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/troubleshooting/common-issues/).
:::

```

`docs/src/content/docs/operations/auto-update.md`:

```md
---
title: Auto-Update & Releases
description: How the toolkit updates itself, and how to cut a release.
---

## How auto-update works

On every bare launch (not during `--check` or other diagnostic flags), the toolkit:

1. Queries `https://api.github.com/repos/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/releases/latest`, with a 5-second timeout. Any failure (offline, API down, no release yet) is silent — a normal launch never blocks on network issues.
2. Compares the release tag against the running binary's version **numerically** (`0.10.0` is correctly newer than `0.9.0` — a plain string compare would get this wrong).
3. If newer, downloads the release's `apkat.exe` asset, then performs the update:
   - Renames the **currently running** exe aside to `apkat.old.exe` — Windows allows renaming a file that's in use, just not deleting or overwriting it.
   - Moves the newly downloaded exe into the original path.
   - Relaunches from that path and exits the old process.
4. On the **next** launch, the leftover `apkat.old.exe` (no longer locked by anything) is deleted — the one file Windows wouldn't let the update process remove mid-flight.

No new dependency was added for this — GitHub's release JSON is parsed with targeted substring scanning rather than a full JSON parser, since the shape being read (`tag_name`, and the `browser_download_url` of the one asset named `apkat.exe`) is narrow and stable. Covered by unit tests against a realistic multi-asset release payload.

## Cutting a release

The GitHub Actions workflow at `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds on `windows-latest` and attaches the binary whenever a tag matching `v*` is pushed:

```bash
# bump `version` in Cargo.toml first, then:
git add Cargo.toml
git commit -m "Bump version to 0.2.0"
git tag v0.2.0
git push origin main v0.2.0
```

The workflow produces `apkat.exe` and uploads it as a release asset with that exact name — matching what the auto-updater looks for — alongside `apkat-setup.exe`, the Inno Setup installer meant for first-time installs (see [Installation](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/getting-started/installation/)).

```

`docs/src/content/docs/operations/full-setup.md`:

```md
---
title: Full Setup Pipeline
description: What "Run full setup (template)" actually does, step by step.
---

**Run full setup (template)** chains every other screen's core action into one pass, stopping at the first failure instead of continuing past a broken step.

```
== Full setup: emulator, SSL tooling, mitmproxy sandbox ==
```

1. **Detect.** Lists SDK state, AVDs, and connected devices.
2. **Ensure a rootable device is online.**
   - If a device is already connected, skips straight to step 3.
   - Otherwise, checks whether *any* existing AVD is rootable (not just whether one exists) — if the only AVDs around are Play Store images, it creates a fresh rootable one rather than booting something it already knows won't support the later steps.
   - Launches the AVD and polls `sys.boot_completed`, with periodic heartbeats (`still booting... (45s)`) so a multi-minute boot doesn't look frozen.
3. **SSL tooling.** Installs `frida-tools`/`objection`, then downloads (or reuses the cached) matching `frida-server` and pushes/starts it.
4. **mitmproxy.** Installs if missing, starts `mitmweb` with noise filtering and a valid auth token, sets the device's proxy, then runs the full CA-trust sequence (including the dm-verity reboot cycle if the image needs it).

## Why it stops on failure

Early versions of this pipeline logged a step's failure but kept going into the next, unrelated stage — meaning a failed `frida-server` push would be followed by mitmproxy setup starting anyway, burying the real problem in noise. Every step in the chain now returns a plain success/failure signal, and the pipeline checks it:

```
Stopped: couldn't push/start frida-server. Fix the error above, then re-run.
```

Fix whatever it reports and re-run — already-completed steps (an already-installed tool, an already-booted device) are detected and skipped, so re-running is cheap.

```

`docs/src/content/docs/operations/multi-device.md`:

```md
---
title: Multi-Device Selection
description: How the toolkit handles more than one connected ADB device.
---

## The problem

Every `adb` command this tool runs needs to know which device to target. With exactly one device attached, there's no ambiguity. With two or more — a real device plus an emulator, or a stale/"ghost" device entry left behind by a previous session — a bare `adb shell ...` fails outright:

```
adb.exe: more than one device/emulator
```

This happened repeatedly during development: closing and relaunching an AVD can leave an old entry behind in `adb devices` that never really went away, and running `adb root` restarts `adbd`, which causes a brief disconnect/reconnect that can confuse naive device selection mid-sequence.

## How it's handled

Every `adb` call in the toolkit goes through a shared targeting function that:

1. Uses a **manually pinned device** if you've set one (see below).
2. Otherwise prefers the first device actually in `"device"` state (online), falling back to whatever's listed if none are, so an explicit `-s <serial>` always avoids the ambiguity error — even against a ghost/offline entry.

For sequences that restart `adbd` mid-flight (rooting, then remounting, then pushing a cert), the device serial is **resolved once and pinned for the whole sequence**, so a device-list hiccup during the `adb root` reconnect window can't make a later step silently land on a different device than the one that was just rooted.

## Select ADB device

From the main menu, lists every entry `adb devices` reports (serial + state), plus an **Auto** option to clear any manual pin and go back to the automatic selection above. Your choice applies globally to every screen until changed.

```

`docs/src/content/docs/ssl-pinning/bypass-and-repin.md`:

```md
---
title: Bypass & Re-pin
description: Installing Frida tooling, pushing frida-server, bypassing pinning, and reversing it.
---

## Install frida-tools & objection

Installs both via `pip` if either is missing, streaming install progress live. Idempotent — if already present, just reports the installed version.

## Push & start frida-server

1. Reads the host's `frida --version` and the connected device's ABI (`adb shell getprop ro.product.cpu.abi`), mapping Android ABI names to Frida's naming (`arm64-v8a` → `arm64`, etc).
2. Downloads the matching `frida-server-<version>-android-<abi>.xz` build from GitHub — **cached** at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\apk-toolkit\cache`, so re-running this doesn't redownload on every pass.
3. Decompresses it (`xz`/`unxz`, auto-discovered even if not on the invoking shell's `PATH` — see [Troubleshooting](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/troubleshooting/common-issues/)).
4. Pushes it to `/data/local/tmp/frida-server`, `chmod`s it executable, and starts it in the background.

`frida-server` can start unprivileged (as the `shell` user), but it can only **spawn or attach to other processes with root** — this is enforced by Android, not by this tool.

## Bypass pinning for a package

Opens the package picker (see [Listing & Filtering](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/app-manager/listing-and-filtering/)) — each entry is annotated `[bypass active]` if this tool already has a tracked session running against it. On selection, it:

```
frida -D <serial> -f <package> -c akabe1/frida-multiple-unpinning
```

- Targets the device by **explicit serial** (`-D`, resolved the same way every other `adb` call in this tool is), not Frida's bare `-U` — with more than one device attached, `-U` picks arbitrarily and can silently hang spawning against a device that has no `frida-server` running.
- Fetches the well-known universal SSL-unpinning script live from Frida's CodeShare (`akabe1/frida-multiple-unpinning`) — no local script file to manage.
- Requires root on the device; see [AVDs & Rooting](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/emulator/avds-and-rooting/).

## Stop bypass / re-pin a package

Lists only packages with an active tracked session. On selection:

1. Kills the tracked `frida` process by PID.
2. Force-stops the app (`adb shell am force-stop <pkg>`) so its *next* launch has no injected agent — this is what actually restores normal pinned behavior, since killing the Frida client alone doesn't un-hook an already-running process.

If no session is tracked for a package (e.g. Frida already exited on its own), it says so rather than silently no-op'ing.

```

`docs/src/content/docs/static-analysis/apktool.md`:

```md
---
title: APK Tool (apktool)
description: Decompiling and recompiling an APK, with the output kept in the repo's own workspace.
---

apktool decodes an APK's resources and `AndroidManifest.xml` back to (near-)source form, and can rebuild an edited copy — useful for inspecting resources, tampering with the manifest, or patching smali directly, which JADX's Java-focused view doesn't cover.

## Install

apktool ships as a single jar with no installer. **Install/verify** checks a local cache (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\apk-toolkit\cache`) for `apktool_*.jar`; if missing, it fetches the latest release from [iBotPeaches/Apktool](https://github.com/iBotPeaches/Apktool) on GitHub and downloads the jar — a one-time ~15MB download, with live progress. Requires a JRE (checked via `java -version`); if Java isn't installed, it says so rather than failing cryptically mid-download.

## Decompile APK (pick package)

Opens the same package picker used throughout the app (see [Listing & Filtering](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/app-manager/listing-and-filtering/)). On selection:

1. Pulls the package's APK via `adb pull` into `workspace/apktool/<package>/<package>.apk` — **in the repo's own `workspace/` directory**, not a hidden cache, so it's easy to find and the folder is already `.gitignore`d.
2. Runs `apktool d <apk> -o workspace/apktool/<package>/decompiled -f`.
3. Opens the resulting `decompiled/` folder directly in File Explorer.

```
workspace/apktool/com.example.app/
├── com.example.app.apk
└── decompiled/
    ├── AndroidManifest.xml
    ├── res/
    ├── smali/
    └── ...
```

## Compile APK (from folder)

Takes a folder path (typically one of the `decompiled/` directories above, possibly edited) and runs `apktool b <folder>`, which writes the rebuilt APK to `<folder>/dist/`. Opens that output folder when done.

:::note
A compiled APK from apktool is unsigned — install it with `adb install -r` as-is only works on a device that doesn't enforce signature verification strictly in your workflow, or re-sign it (`apksigner`) before installing on a stricter device.
:::

```

`docs/src/content/docs/static-analysis/jadx.md`:

```md
---
title: JADX
description: Decompiling Java/Kotlin sources from an APK and opening them directly in jadx-gui.
---

JADX decompiles DEX bytecode back to readable Java, which is usually a faster way to understand an app's actual logic than reading smali from apktool's output.

## Install

Checked/installed automatically the first time you use it: downloads the latest [skylot/jadx](https://github.com/skylot/jadx) release and extracts it into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\apk-toolkit\cache\jadx`. Requires a JRE (checked via `java -version`).

JADX's releases ship several similarly-named assets — the full cross-platform bundle (`jadx-<version>.zip`, containing both the `jadx` CLI and `jadx-gui`) alongside GUI-only platform builds (`jadx-gui-<version>-win.zip`, and macOS `.dmg`s). The installer matches the **exact** full-bundle filename once it knows the release's version number, rather than a loose substring match — a naive "contains jadx-" check would just as happily match the Windows GUI-only zip.

Extraction uses Windows' built-in `tar` (the real `bsdtar` shipped in `System32`, not whatever `tar` a shell's own `PATH` might resolve to first — see [Troubleshooting](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/troubleshooting/common-issues/) for why that distinction matters).

## Open package in JADX

Opens the package picker, pulls the selected package's APK into `workspace/jadx/<package>/<package>.apk` (same repo-local workspace convention as apktool), and launches `jadx-gui.bat` directly with that file — no separate "open file" step needed in the GUI.

```

`docs/src/content/docs/static-analysis/mt-manager.md`:

```md
---
title: MT Manager
description: Getting MT Manager (an on-device file/APK manager) installed on the emulator.
---

[MT Manager](https://mt-manager.en.uptodown.com/android) is a popular Android file manager with built-in APK editing, signing, and root-file-browsing features — useful to have installed *inside* the sandbox for on-device poking around, alongside the host-side tools (apktool, JADX) covered elsewhere in Static Analysis Setup.

## Why this isn't fully automatic

Unlike the other tools in this toolkit, MT Manager isn't fetched from a stable, scrapable URL. Its distributor (uptodown.com) serves the actual file through a JS/AJAX-driven download flow rather than a fixed link — confirmed by testing directly against several of their known endpoint patterns during development, none of which returned a working file URL from a plain HTTP request.

Shipping a scraper against an undocumented, frontend-driven endpoint would silently break the moment uptodown changes their page — and "download whatever this regex found" is also just a worse security posture for a tool meant to help with exactly this kind of app-distribution scrutiny.

## What it does instead

**MT Manager (install on device)** opens uptodown's MT Manager download page in your default browser. From there:

1. Download the APK manually.
2. Use **App manager → Install APK**, pointing it at the downloaded file.

Both of those are the same, already-tested code paths used for every other APK install in this toolkit.

```

`docs/src/content/docs/troubleshooting/common-issues.md`:

```md
---
title: Common Issues
description: Real problems found and fixed during development, and why they happened.
---

These are genuine bugs this toolkit hit (and fixed) while being built against a real Android SDK install and a real emulator — not hypotheticals. Documented here because the root causes are useful to understand even if you never touch this codebase.

## "more than one device/emulator"

**Symptom:** any `adb` command fails outright once a second device entry appears — including stale "ghost" entries left behind by a previous emulator session that never fully cleared.

**Cause:** `adb` refuses to guess which device you mean once more than one is listed, even if only one is actually online.

**Fix:** every `adb` call is routed through a shared targeting function that resolves `-s <serial>` explicitly — see [Multi-Device Selection](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/operations/multi-device/).

## `avdmanager` always reports "not found"

**Symptom:** `avdmanager` is genuinely installed and working, but a presence check using `avdmanager --version` always fails.

**Cause:** this version of `avdmanager` has no `--version` flag at all — it exits with status 1 and a usage error on it. A presence check based on exit status silently misreports it as missing.

**Fix:** use `avdmanager list` (a harmless, always-zero-exit no-op) as the presence probe instead.

## mitmweb's web UI returns 403

**Symptom:** the browser opens (or you open it manually) to `http://127.0.0.1:8081/`, and get a 403.

**Cause:** mitmweb requires a `?token=` query parameter (or an `Authorization: Bearer` header) unless you explicitly disable auth. The bare URL has no token.

**Fix:** generate a token, pass it via `--set web_password=<token>`, and open the browser at the fully-qualified URL (`?token=<token>`) yourself — see [Proxy & CA Certificate](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/mitmproxy/proxy-and-cert/).

## `remount` reports success, but pushing to `/system` still fails with "Read-only file system"

**Symptom:** `adb root` and `adb remount` both report `[OK]`, but writing to `/system/etc/security/cacerts/` fails anyway.

**Cause:** on a **dm-verity-enabled image**, the first `remount` doesn't actually make `/system` writable — it disables verity and requires a **reboot** before a second `remount` will succeed. The real remount output (`Disabling verity for /system — Now reboot your device for settings to take effect`) makes this obvious once you look at it, but a wrapper that only reports a bare success/failure status hides it completely.

**Fix:** detect that exact message, reboot, wait for boot, then root + remount again — the second attempt reports `remount succeeded` for real. See [Proxy & CA Certificate](/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/mitmproxy/proxy-and-cert/).

## Chrome shows "No internet" after setting up the proxy

**Symptom:** the device's system proxy is set correctly and mitmweb is running, but Chrome shows "No internet," and some pages partially fail to load.

**Cause:** Android's connectivity check runs over **HTTPS**. If the device's proxy is set but mitmproxy's CA isn't yet trusted (see the dm-verity issue above), every HTTPS connection — including that connectivity check — fails with a certificate error. Plain HTTP content, which needs no certificate validation, can still partially load, which is why it looks like a partial rather than total outage.

**Fix:** this resolves itself once the CA-trust sequence actually completes (see above) — it's a symptom of an incomplete cert install, not a separate bug.

## `xz`/`openssl` reported missing despite being installed

**Symptom:** a tool is genuinely on the machine (confirmed via `Get-Command` in PowerShell) but the toolkit reports it missing.

**Cause:** tools bundled with Git for Windows (`xz`, `openssl`, `curl`) live under Git's own `mingw64/bin`/`usr/bin`, which isn't always on every shell's `PATH` even when it's on the "real" system `PATH` — this varies by which shell launched the process.

**Fix:** fall back to checking Git for Windows' known install locations directly, the same pattern already used for Android SDK tool discovery, rather than relying solely on `PATH`.

## Pasted install paths with quotes fail

**Symptom:** `Install APK` fails when the path was copied via Windows' "Copy as path," which wraps the result in literal double quotes.

**Fix:** strip matching leading/trailing quote characters before using the path.

```

`installer.iss`:

```iss
; Inno Setup script for APK Analysis Toolkit (apkat).
; Installs per-user (no admin needed) and adds the install dir to the
; user's PATH so "apkat" works from any shell after install.
#define MyAppName "APK Analysis Toolkit"
#define MyAppExeName "apkat.exe"
#ifndef MyAppVersion
  #define MyAppVersion "0.0.0"
#endif

[Setup]
AppId={{B6B2B6B4-6B0A-4B7E-9C7B-APKAT00000001}
AppName={#MyAppName}
AppVersion={#MyAppVersion}
DefaultDirName={autopf}\APK Analysis Toolkit
DefaultGroupName=APK Analysis Toolkit
PrivilegesRequired=lowest
OutputBaseFilename=apkat-setup
OutputDir=installer-out
Compression=lzma2
SolidCompression=yes
ArchitecturesInstallIn64BitMode=x64compatible
DisableProgramGroupPage=yes

[Files]
Source: "target\release\{#MyAppExeName}"; DestDir: "{app}"; Flags: ignoreversion

[Icons]
Name: "{group}\APK Analysis Toolkit"; Filename: "{app}\{#MyAppExeName}"

[Code]
const
  EnvKey = 'Environment';
  HWND_BROADCAST = $FFFF;
  WM_SETTINGCHANGE = $1A;
  SMTO_ABORTIFHUNG = 2;

function SendMessageTimeout(hWnd: LongInt; Msg: LongInt; wParam: LongInt;
  lParam: string; fuFlags: LongInt; uTimeout: LongInt; var lpdwResult: LongInt): LongInt;
  external 'SendMessageTimeoutW@user32.dll stdcall';

procedure BroadcastEnvChange();
var
  Dummy: LongInt;
begin
  SendMessageTimeout(HWND_BROADCAST, WM_SETTINGCHANGE, 0, 'Environment', SMTO_ABORTIFHUNG, 1000, Dummy);
end;

procedure AddToPath();
var
  Path: string;
begin
  if not RegQueryStringValue(HKEY_CURRENT_USER, EnvKey, 'Path', Path) then
    Path := '';
  if Pos(LowerCase(ExpandConstant('{app}') + ';'), LowerCase(Path + ';')) = 0 then begin
    if (Path <> '') and (Path[Length(Path)] <> ';') then
      Path := Path + ';';
    Path := Path + ExpandConstant('{app}');
    RegWriteStringValue(HKEY_CURRENT_USER, EnvKey, 'Path', Path);
    BroadcastEnvChange();
  end;
end;

procedure RemoveFromPath();
var
  Path, Entry: string;
  P: Integer;
begin
  if not RegQueryStringValue(HKEY_CURRENT_USER, EnvKey, 'Path', Path) then
    exit;
  Entry := ExpandConstant('{app}');
  P := Pos(LowerCase(Entry + ';'), LowerCase(Path + ';'));
  if P > 0 then begin
    Delete(Path, P, Length(Entry) + 1);
    RegWriteStringValue(HKEY_CURRENT_USER, EnvKey, 'Path', Path);
    BroadcastEnvChange();
  end;
end;

procedure CurStepChanged(CurStep: TSetupStep);
begin
  if CurStep = ssPostInstall then
    AddToPath();
end;

procedure CurUninstallStepChanged(CurUninstallStep: TUninstallStep);
begin
  if CurUninstallStep = usPostUninstall then
    RemoveFromPath();
end;

[UninstallDelete]
Type: files; Name: "{app}\apkat.old.exe"
Type: files; Name: "{app}\apkat.new.exe"

```

`src/main.rs`:

```rs
mod modules;
mod util;

use std::io;
use std::panic;
use std::sync::mpsc::{self, Receiver, Sender};
use std::thread;
use std::time::Duration;

use crossterm::{
    event::{self, Event, KeyCode, KeyEventKind},
    execute,
    terminal::{disable_raw_mode, enable_raw_mode, EnterAlternateScreen, LeaveAlternateScreen},
};
use ratatui::{
    backend::{Backend, CrosstermBackend},
    layout::{Constraint, Direction, Layout},
    style::{Color, Modifier, Style},
    text::{Line, Text},
    widgets::{Block, Borders, List, ListItem, ListState, Paragraph, Wrap},
    Frame, Terminal,
};

use modules::{apktool, apps, emulator, jadx, mitm, mt_manager, ssl_pin, template, update};
use util::shell;

const SPINNER: [&str; 10] = ["⠋", "⠙", "⠹", "⠸", "⠼", "⠴", "⠦", "⠧", "⠇", "⠏"];
const VERSION: &str = env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION");

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
enum Screen {
    Main,
    Static,
    Dynamic,
    Emulator,
    SslPin,
    Mitm,
    Apps,
    ApkTool,
    Jadx,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
enum PendingAction {
    SslBypass,
    StopBypass,
    PackageInfo,
    Launch,
    InstallApk,
    Uninstall,
    Disable,
    Enable,
    SelectDevice,
    ApktoolDecompile,
    ApktoolCompile,
    JadxOpen,
}

const MAIN_ITEMS: [&str; 5] = [
    "App manager (adb)",
    "Static Analysis Setup",
    "Dynamic Analysis Setup",
    "Select ADB device",
    "Quit",
];
const STATIC_ITEMS: [&str; 4] = ["APK tool (apktool)", "JADX", "MT Manager (install on device)", "Back"];
const DYNAMIC_ITEMS: [&str; 5] = [
    "Run full setup (template)",
    "Emulator",
    "SSL pinning bypass",
    "mitmproxy",
    "Back",
];
const APKTOOL_ITEMS: [&str; 3] = ["Decompile APK (pick package)", "Compile APK (from folder)", "Back"];
const JADX_ITEMS: [&str; 2] = ["Open package in JADX", "Back"];
const EMULATOR_ITEMS: [&str; 6] = [
    "Detect & list AVDs",
    "Create new AVD (if none exist)",
    "Launch existing AVD (writable system)",
    "Root device (adb root)",
    "De-root device (adb unroot)",
    "Back",
];
const SSLPIN_ITEMS: [&str; 5] = [
    "Install frida-tools & objection",
    "Push & start frida-server on device",
    "Bypass pinning for a package",
    "Stop bypass / re-pin a package",
    "Back",
];
const MITM_ITEMS: [&str; 4] = [
    "Install mitmproxy",
    "Start proxy & point device at it",
    "Wait for cert & install as system CA",
    "Back",
];
const APPS_ITEMS: [&str; 8] = [
    "List installed packages",
    "Package info (detailed report)",
    "Launch package",
    "Install APK",
    "Uninstall package",
    "Disable package",
    "Enable package",
    "Back",
];

struct AwaitingInput {
    prompt: &'static str,
    buffer: String,
    action: PendingAction,
}

struct Picker {
    items: Vec<String>,
    labels: Vec<String>,
    filter: String,
    state: ListState,
    action: PendingAction,
}

impl Picker {
    /// Indices into `items`/`labels` matching the current filter (all of
    /// them if the filter is empty) — lets you type a package name instead
    /// of scrolling through a long list to find it.
    fn visible(&self) -> Vec<usize> {
        if self.filter.is_empty() {
            return (0..self.items.len()).collect();
        }
        let needle = self.filter.to_lowercase();
        self.labels
            .iter()
            .enumerate()
            .filter(|(_, l)| l.to_lowercase().contains(&needle))
            .map(|(i, _)| i)
            .collect()
    }
}

struct App {
    screen: Screen,
    state: ListState,
    log: Vec<String>,
    log_scroll: u16,
    input: Option<AwaitingInput>,
    picker: Option<Picker>,
    job: Option<Receiver<String>>,
    busy: bool,
    spinner: usize,
}

fn wrap_index(current: usize, delta: i32, len: usize) -> usize {
    let len = len as i32;
    let i = current as i32;
    (((i + delta) % len + len) % len) as usize
}

impl App {
    fn items(&self) -> &'static [&'static str] {
        match self.screen {
            Screen::Main => &MAIN_ITEMS,
            Screen::Static => &STATIC_ITEMS,
            Screen::Dynamic => &DYNAMIC_ITEMS,
            Screen::Emulator => &EMULATOR_ITEMS,
            Screen::SslPin => &SSLPIN_ITEMS,
            Screen::Mitm => &MITM_ITEMS,
            Screen::Apps => &APPS_ITEMS,
            Screen::ApkTool => &APKTOOL_ITEMS,
            Screen::Jadx => &JADX_ITEMS,
        }
    }

    fn title(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self.screen {
            Screen::Main => "APK Analysis Toolkit",
            Screen::Static => "Static Analysis Setup",
            Screen::Dynamic => "Dynamic Analysis Setup",
            Screen::Emulator => "Emulator",
            Screen::SslPin => "SSL Pinning Bypass",
            Screen::Mitm => "mitmproxy",
            Screen::Apps => "App Manager (adb)",
            Screen::ApkTool => "APK Tool",
            Screen::Jadx => "JADX",
        }
    }

    fn move_sel(&mut self, delta: i32) {
        let len = self.items().len();
        let i = self.state.selected().unwrap_or(0);
        self.state.select(Some(wrap_index(i, delta, len)));
    }

    fn reset_sel(&mut self) {
        self.state.select(Some(0));
    }

    fn go_back(&mut self) {
        self.screen = match self.screen {
            Screen::ApkTool | Screen::Jadx => Screen::Static,
            Screen::Emulator | Screen::SslPin | Screen::Mitm => Screen::Dynamic,
            _ => Screen::Main, // includes Apps — now reached directly from Main
        };
        self.reset_sel();
    }

    /// Runs `job` on a background thread, streaming its log lines in live.
    /// ponytail: one job at a time (blocked below) — per-screen job queues
    /// are the upgrade if parallel actions are ever needed.
    fn start_job<F>(&mut self, job: F)
    where
        F: FnOnce(Sender<String>) + Send + 'static,
    {
        let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
        self.log.clear();
        self.log_scroll = 0;
        self.job = Some(rx);
        self.busy = true;
        thread::spawn(move || job(tx));
    }

    fn poll_job(&mut self) {
        let Some(rx) = &self.job else { return };
        loop {
            match rx.try_recv() {
                // A leading \r means "replace the last line" — used for
                // live download/install progress bars that update in place.
                Ok(line) => match line.strip_prefix('\r') {
                    Some(text) => match self.log.last_mut() {
                        Some(last) => *last = text.to_string(),
                        None => self.log.push(text.to_string()),
                    },
                    None => self.log.push(line),
                },
                Err(mpsc::TryRecvError::Empty) => break,
                Err(mpsc::TryRecvError::Disconnected) => {
                    self.busy = false;
                    self.job = None;
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /// Returns true if the app should quit.
    fn select(&mut self) -> bool {
        if self.busy {
            return false;
        }
        let i = self.state.selected().unwrap_or(0);
        match self.screen {
            Screen::Main => match i {
                0 => {
                    self.screen = Screen::Apps;
                    self.reset_sel();
                }
                1 => {
                    self.screen = Screen::Static;
                    self.reset_sel();
                }
                2 => {
                    self.screen = Screen::Dynamic;
                    self.reset_sel();
                }
                3 => self.open_picker(PendingAction::SelectDevice),
                4 => return true,
                _ => {}
            },
            Screen::Static => match i {
                0 => {
                    self.screen = Screen::ApkTool;
                    self.reset_sel();
                }
                1 => {
                    self.screen = Screen::Jadx;
                    self.reset_sel();
                }
                2 => self.start_job(|tx| mt_manager::install(&tx)),
                3 => self.go_back(),
                _ => {}
            },
            Screen::Dynamic => match i {
                0 => self.start_job(|tx| template::run(&tx)),
                1 => {
                    self.screen = Screen::Emulator;
                    self.reset_sel();
                }
                2 => {
                    self.screen = Screen::SslPin;
                    self.reset_sel();
                }
                3 => {
                    self.screen = Screen::Mitm;
                    self.reset_sel();
                }
                4 => self.go_back(),
                _ => {}
            },
            Screen::ApkTool => match i {
                0 => self.open_picker(PendingAction::ApktoolDecompile),
                1 => self.ask("Decompiled folder path: ", PendingAction::ApktoolCompile),
                2 => self.go_back(),
                _ => {}
            },
            Screen::Jadx => match i {
                0 => self.open_picker(PendingAction::JadxOpen),
                1 => self.go_back(),
                _ => {}
            },
            Screen::Emulator => match i {
                0 => self.start_job(|tx| emulator::detect(&tx)),
                1 => self.start_job(|tx| { emulator::create_avd(&tx); }),
                2 => self.start_job(|tx| { emulator::launch_avd(&tx); }),
                3 => self.start_job(|tx| emulator::root_device(&tx)),
                4 => self.start_job(|tx| emulator::deroot_device(&tx)),
                5 => self.go_back(),
                _ => {}
            },
            Screen::SslPin => match i {
                0 => self.start_job(|tx| { ssl_pin::install_tools(&tx); }),
                1 => self.start_job(|tx| { ssl_pin::push_frida_server(&tx); }),
                2 => self.open_picker(PendingAction::SslBypass),
                3 => self.open_picker(PendingAction::StopBypass),
                4 => self.go_back(),
                _ => {}
            },
            Screen::Mitm => match i {
                0 => self.start_job(|tx| { mitm::install(&tx); }),
                1 => self.start_job(|tx| { mitm::start_and_set_proxy(&tx); }),
                2 => self.start_job(|tx| { mitm::install_cert(&tx); }),
                3 => self.go_back(),
                _ => {}
            },
            Screen::Apps => match i {
                0 => self.start_job(|tx| apps::list_packages(&tx)),
                1 => self.open_picker(PendingAction::PackageInfo),
                2 => self.open_picker(PendingAction::Launch),
                3 => self.ask("APK path: ", PendingAction::InstallApk),
                4 => self.open_picker(PendingAction::Uninstall),
                5 => self.open_picker(PendingAction::Disable),
                6 => self.open_picker(PendingAction::Enable),
                7 => self.go_back(),
                _ => {}
            },
        }
        false
    }

    fn ask(&mut self, prompt: &'static str, action: PendingAction) {
        self.input = Some(AwaitingInput {
            prompt,
            buffer: String::new(),
            action,
        });
    }

    /// Lists installed packages (or active bypass sessions) and lets the user
    /// pick one instead of typing a name. Annotates bypass status so it's
    /// visible which apps already have an active unpinning session running.
    fn open_picker(&mut self, action: PendingAction) {
        let (items, labels): (Vec<String>, Vec<String>) = match action {
            PendingAction::StopBypass => {
                let items = ssl_pin::active_bypasses();
                if items.is_empty() {
                    self.log = vec!["[!] No active bypass sessions tracked by this tool.".into()];
                    return;
                }
                let labels = items.clone();
                (items, labels)
            }
            PendingAction::SelectDevice => {
                let mut items = vec![String::new()];
                let mut labels = vec!["Auto (let the tool pick)".to_string()];
                for (serial, state) in shell::adb_devices() {
                    items.push(serial.clone());
                    labels.push(format!("{serial}  ({state})"));
                }
                (items, labels)
            }
            _ => {
                let items = apps::packages();
                if items.is_empty() {
                    self.log = vec!["[FAIL] No device connected, or no packages found.".into()];
                    return;
                }
                let labels = items
                    .iter()
                    .map(|p| {
                        if action == PendingAction::SslBypass && ssl_pin::is_bypassed(p) {
                            format!("{p}  [bypass active]")
                        } else {
                            p.clone()
                        }
                    })
                    .collect();
                (items, labels)
            }
        };
        let mut state = ListState::default();
        state.select(Some(0));
        self.picker = Some(Picker { items, labels, filter: String::new(), state, action });
    }
}

fn restore_terminal() {
    let _ = disable_raw_mode();
    let _ = execute!(io::stdout(), LeaveAlternateScreen);
}

fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    // Only auto-update on a bare launch — never during --check or any other
    // flag-driven diagnostic run, so those stay fast and offline-safe.
    if std::env::args().count() == 1 {
        update::check_and_apply(VERSION);
    }

    if std::env::args().any(|a| a == "--check") {
        let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
        thread::spawn(move || emulator::detect(&tx));
        for line in rx.iter() {
            println!("{line}");
        }
        return Ok(());
    }

    let default_hook = panic::take_hook();
    panic::set_hook(Box::new(move |info| {
        restore_terminal();
        default_hook(info);
    }));

    enable_raw_mode()?;
    execute!(io::stdout(), EnterAlternateScreen)?;
    let backend = CrosstermBackend::new(io::stdout());
    let mut terminal = Terminal::new(backend)?;
    terminal.clear()?;

    let mut app = App {
        screen: Screen::Main,
        state: ListState::default(),
        log: vec!["↑/↓ or j/k move · Enter select · Esc/Backspace back · q quit".into()],
        log_scroll: 0,
        input: None,
        picker: None,
        job: None,
        busy: false,
        spinner: 0,
    };
    app.reset_sel();

    let result = run_loop(&mut terminal, &mut app);

    restore_terminal();
    result
}

fn run_loop<B: Backend>(terminal: &mut Terminal<B>, app: &mut App) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    loop {
        app.poll_job();
        if app.busy {
            app.spinner = (app.spinner + 1) % SPINNER.len();
        }

        terminal.draw(|f| draw(f, app))?;

        if !event::poll(Duration::from_millis(100))? {
            continue;
        }
        let Event::Key(key) = event::read()? else { continue };
        if key.kind != KeyEventKind::Press {
            continue;
        }

        if let Some(picker) = &mut app.picker {
            let visible = picker.visible();
            match key.code {
                KeyCode::Esc => app.picker = None,
                KeyCode::Down => {
                    let len = visible.len();
                    if len > 0 {
                        let i = picker.state.selected().unwrap_or(0);
                        picker.state.select(Some(wrap_index(i, 1, len)));
                    }
                }
                KeyCode::Up => {
                    let len = visible.len();
                    if len > 0 {
                        let i = picker.state.selected().unwrap_or(0);
                        picker.state.select(Some(wrap_index(i, -1, len)));
                    }
                }
                // Typing narrows the list instead of scrolling it — j/k are no
                // longer vim-bound here since they're valid package-name letters.
                KeyCode::Char(c) => {
                    picker.filter.push(c);
                    picker.state.select(Some(0));
                }
                KeyCode::Backspace => {
                    picker.filter.pop();
                    picker.state.select(Some(0));
                }
                KeyCode::Enter => {
                    let Some(&real_idx) = visible.get(picker.state.selected().unwrap_or(0)) else {
                        continue;
                    };
                    let pkg = picker.items[real_idx].clone();
                    let action = picker.action;
                    app.picker = None;
                    match action {
                        PendingAction::SslBypass => app.start_job(move |tx| ssl_pin::bypass(&pkg, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::StopBypass => app.start_job(move |tx| ssl_pin::stop_bypass(&pkg, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::Uninstall => app.start_job(move |tx| { apps::uninstall(&pkg, &tx); }),
                        PendingAction::Disable => app.start_job(move |tx| { apps::disable(&pkg, &tx); }),
                        PendingAction::Enable => app.start_job(move |tx| { apps::enable(&pkg, &tx); }),
                        PendingAction::PackageInfo => app.start_job(move |tx| apps::package_info(&pkg, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::Launch => app.start_job(move |tx| apps::launch(&pkg, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::ApktoolDecompile => app.start_job(move |tx| apktool::decompile_package(&pkg, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::JadxOpen => app.start_job(move |tx| jadx::open_package(&pkg, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::SelectDevice => {
                            if pkg.is_empty() {
                                shell::set_selected_device(None);
                                app.log = vec!["[OK] cleared manual device selection — auto-picking again.".into()];
                            } else {
                                shell::set_selected_device(Some(pkg.clone()));
                                app.log = vec![format!("[OK] now targeting {pkg} for all adb actions.")];
                            }
                        }
                        PendingAction::InstallApk | PendingAction::ApktoolCompile => {} // free-text-only
                    }
                }
                _ => {}
            }
            continue;
        }

        if let Some(input) = &mut app.input {
            match key.code {
                KeyCode::Esc => app.input = None,
                KeyCode::Enter => {
                    let value = input.buffer.clone();
                    let action = input.action;
                    app.input = None;
                    match action {
                        PendingAction::SslBypass => app.start_job(move |tx| ssl_pin::bypass(&value, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::InstallApk => app.start_job(move |tx| apps::install_apk(&value, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::Uninstall => app.start_job(move |tx| apps::uninstall(&value, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::Disable => app.start_job(move |tx| apps::disable(&value, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::Enable => app.start_job(move |tx| apps::enable(&value, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::ApktoolCompile => app.start_job(move |tx| apktool::compile_folder(&value, &tx)),
                        PendingAction::StopBypass
                        | PendingAction::PackageInfo
                        | PendingAction::Launch
                        | PendingAction::SelectDevice
                        | PendingAction::ApktoolDecompile
                        | PendingAction::JadxOpen => {} // picker-only
                    }
                }
                KeyCode::Backspace => {
                    input.buffer.pop();
                }
                KeyCode::Char(c) => input.buffer.push(c),
                _ => {}
            }
            continue;
        }

        match key.code {
            KeyCode::Char('q') => return Ok(()),
            KeyCode::Down | KeyCode::Char('j') => app.move_sel(1),
            KeyCode::Up | KeyCode::Char('k') => app.move_sel(-1),
            KeyCode::PageDown => app.log_scroll = app.log_scroll.saturating_add(10),
            KeyCode::PageUp => app.log_scroll = app.log_scroll.saturating_sub(10),
            KeyCode::Char('c') => {
                let report = app.log.join("\n");
                match shell::copy_to_clipboard(&report) {
                    Ok(()) => app.log.push("[OK] copied to clipboard".into()),
                    Err(e) => app.log.push(format!("[FAIL] clipboard: {e}")),
                }
            }
            KeyCode::Esc | KeyCode::Backspace => {
                if app.screen != Screen::Main {
                    app.go_back();
                }
            }
            KeyCode::Enter if app.select() => return Ok(()),
            KeyCode::Enter => {}
            _ => {}
        }
    }
}

fn draw(f: &mut Frame, app: &mut App) {
    let outer = Layout::default()
        .direction(Direction::Vertical)
        .constraints([Constraint::Min(3), Constraint::Length(1), Constraint::Length(1)])
        .split(f.area());

    let chunks = Layout::default()
        .direction(Direction::Horizontal)
        .constraints([Constraint::Percentage(35), Constraint::Percentage(65)])
        .split(outer[0]);

    if let Some(picker) = &mut app.picker {
        let visible = picker.visible();
        let items: Vec<ListItem> = visible.iter().map(|&i| ListItem::new(picker.labels[i].as_str())).collect();
        let title = if picker.filter.is_empty() {
            "Select a package (type to filter)".to_string()
        } else {
            format!("Select a package — filter: {}_  ({} match{})", picker.filter, visible.len(), if visible.len() == 1 { "" } else { "es" })
        };
        let list = List::new(items)
            .block(Block::default().borders(Borders::ALL).title(title))
            .highlight_style(Style::default().add_modifier(Modifier::BOLD).fg(Color::Green))
            .highlight_symbol("> ");
        f.render_stateful_widget(list, chunks[0], &mut picker.state);
    } else {
        let items: Vec<ListItem> = app.items().iter().map(|i| ListItem::new(*i)).collect();
        let list = List::new(items)
            .block(Block::default().borders(Borders::ALL).title(app.title()))
            .highlight_style(Style::default().add_modifier(Modifier::BOLD).fg(Color::Green))
            .highlight_symbol("> ");
        f.render_stateful_widget(list, chunks[0], &mut app.state);
    }

    let log_title = if app.busy {
        format!("Log {} running... (PgUp/PgDn scroll, c copy)", SPINNER[app.spinner])
    } else {
        "Log (PgUp/PgDn scroll, c copy)".to_string()
    };
    let visible_height = chunks[1].height.saturating_sub(2); // minus top/bottom border
    let max_scroll = (app.log.len() as u16).saturating_sub(visible_height);
    app.log_scroll = app.log_scroll.min(max_scroll);

    let lines: Vec<Line> = app.log.iter().map(|l| style_log_line(l)).collect();
    let log_widget = Paragraph::new(Text::from(lines))
        .block(Block::default().borders(Borders::ALL).title(log_title))
        .wrap(Wrap { trim: false })
        .scroll((app.log_scroll, 0));
    f.render_widget(log_widget, chunks[1]);

    let help = if app.picker.is_some() {
        "↑/↓ move · type to filter · Backspace delete · Enter choose · Esc cancel".to_string()
    } else {
        match &app.input {
            Some(input) => format!("{}{}_", input.prompt, input.buffer),
            None if app.busy => "Running… (Enter is disabled until this finishes; ↑/↓ still work)".to_string(),
            None => "↑/↓ or j/k move · Enter select · Esc/Backspace back · q quit".to_string(),
        }
    };
    f.render_widget(Paragraph::new(help).style(Style::default().fg(Color::DarkGray)), outer[1]);

    // One continuous line of the same dash character throughout, with the
    // label built into it — a separate Block border + overlaid title looked
    // like two mismatched line styles meeting mid-row instead of one rule.
    let label = format!("0xramm────v{VERSION}");
    let width = outer[2].width as usize;
    let rule_len = width.saturating_sub(label.chars().count());
    let footer_line = format!("{}{label}", "─".repeat(rule_len));
    f.render_widget(Paragraph::new(footer_line).style(Style::default().fg(Color::DarkGray)), outer[2]);
}

/// Colors a log line by its status prefix and strips the `==` header markers.
fn style_log_line(line: &str) -> Line<'static> {
    if let Some(inner) = line.strip_prefix("==").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix("==")) {
        return Line::styled(inner.trim().to_string(), Style::default().add_modifier(Modifier::BOLD).fg(Color::Cyan));
    }
    let style = if line.starts_with("[OK]") {
        Style::default().fg(Color::Green)
    } else if line.starts_with("[FAIL]") || line.starts_with("[MISSING]") {
        Style::default().fg(Color::Red)
    } else if line.starts_with("[!]") {
        Style::default().fg(Color::Yellow)
    } else if line.starts_with('>') {
        Style::default().fg(Color::Blue)
    } else if line.starts_with(' ') {
        Style::default().fg(Color::DarkGray)
    } else {
        Style::default()
    };
    Line::styled(line.to_string(), style)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod picker_tests {
    use super::*;

    fn picker(names: &[&str]) -> Picker {
        let items: Vec<String> = names.iter().map(|s| s.to_string()).collect();
        Picker {
            labels: items.clone(),
            items,
            filter: String::new(),
            state: ListState::default(),
            action: PendingAction::Launch,
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn empty_filter_shows_everything_in_order() {
        let p = picker(&["com.android.chrome", "com.example.app", "com.google.gms"]);
        assert_eq!(p.visible(), vec![0, 1, 2]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn filter_narrows_by_substring_case_insensitively() {
        let mut p = picker(&["com.android.chrome", "com.example.app", "com.google.gms"]);
        p.filter = "EXAMPLE".to_string();
        assert_eq!(p.visible(), vec![1]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn filter_matching_nothing_is_empty_not_a_panic() {
        let mut p = picker(&["com.android.chrome"]);
        p.filter = "zzz-no-match".to_string();
        assert!(p.visible().is_empty());
    }
}

`src/modules/apktool.rs`:

```rs
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::sync::mpsc::Sender;

use super::apps;
use crate::util::{cache, github, shell};

const REPO: &str = "iBotPeaches/Apktool";

/// Everything pulled/decompiled/compiled lives under `workspace/` in the
/// current directory — "the local system of this repo", not a hidden cache.
fn workspace_dir() -> PathBuf {
    Path::new("workspace").join("apktool")
}

fn cached_jar() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    std::fs::read_dir(cache::dir()).ok()?.flatten().map(|e| e.path()).find(|p| {
        p.file_name()
            .and_then(|n| n.to_str())
            .map(|n| n.starts_with("apktool_") && n.ends_with(".jar"))
            .unwrap_or(false)
    })
}

/// Ensures apktool.jar is cached locally, downloading the latest release if
/// not. Returns the jar path on success.
pub fn install(tx: &Sender<String>) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    if let Some(jar) = cached_jar() {
        return Some(jar);
    }

    tx.send("== Installing apktool ==".into()).ok();
    if !shell::is_installed("java", "-version") {
        tx.send("[MISSING] Java not found — apktool needs a JRE. Install Java first.".into()).ok();
        return None;
    }

    tx.send("> checking latest apktool release...".into()).ok();
    let Some((version, url)) = github::latest_release_asset(REPO, "apktool_") else {
        tx.send("[FAIL] couldn't reach GitHub to find the latest apktool release.".into()).ok();
        return None;
    };
    let dest = cache::dir().join(format!("apktool_{version}.jar"));
    let dest_str = dest.to_string_lossy().to_string();

    tx.send(format!("> downloading apktool {version}...")).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::run_streaming("curl", &["-L", "-o", &dest_str, &url], tx);
    if !ok {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {out}")).ok();
        return None;
    }
    tx.send(format!("[OK] apktool {version} ready")).ok();
    Some(dest)
}

/// Picks a package from the device, pulls its APK into `workspace/apktool/<pkg>/`,
/// decompiles it there, and opens the result in the file explorer.
pub fn decompile_package(pkg: &str, tx: &Sender<String>) {
    let pkg = pkg.trim();
    tx.send(format!("== Decompiling {pkg} with apktool ==")).ok();

    let Some(jar) = install(tx) else { return };
    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }

    let dest_dir = workspace_dir().join(pkg);
    tx.send("> pulling APK from device...".into()).ok();
    let Some(apk) = apps::pull_apk_to(pkg, &dest_dir, tx) else { return };
    tx.send(format!("[OK] pulled to {}", apk.display())).ok();

    let out_dir = dest_dir.join("decompiled");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&out_dir); // apktool refuses to overwrite without -f; start clean instead

    tx.send("> apktool d ...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::run_streaming(
        "java",
        &["-jar", &jar.to_string_lossy(), "d", &apk.to_string_lossy(), "-o", &out_dir.to_string_lossy(), "-f"],
        tx,
    );
    if !ok {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {out}")).ok();
        return;
    }
    tx.send(format!("[OK] decompiled to {}", out_dir.display())).ok();

    tx.send("> opening in file explorer...".into()).ok();
    let _ = shell::open_url(&out_dir.to_string_lossy());
}

/// Rebuilds a decompiled folder (as produced above, or edited by hand) back into an APK.
pub fn compile_folder(folder: &str, tx: &Sender<String>) {
    let folder = folder.trim().trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'');
    tx.send(format!("== Compiling {folder} with apktool ==")).ok();
    if folder.is_empty() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No folder given.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }

    let Some(jar) = install(tx) else { return };

    tx.send("> apktool b ...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::run_streaming("java", &["-jar", &jar.to_string_lossy(), "b", folder], tx);
    if !ok {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {out}")).ok();
        return;
    }
    let out_apk = Path::new(folder).join("dist");
    tx.send(format!("[OK] compiled — output in {}", out_apk.display())).ok();
    let _ = shell::open_url(&out_apk.to_string_lossy());
}
