`src/modules/apps.rs`:

```rs
use std::sync::mpsc::Sender;

use crate::util::{cache, sdk, shell};

/// Permissions that are individually unremarkable but, combined with other
/// signals (name impersonation, placeholder version, sideloaded), are the
/// building blocks of droppers, overlay/banking trojans, and OTP stealers.
const SENSITIVE_PERMISSIONS: &[(&str, &str)] = &[
    ("INSTALL_PACKAGES", "can silently install other apps (dropper capability)"),
    ("REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES", "can request installing other apps"),
    ("REQUEST_DELETE_PACKAGES", "can request removing other apps"),
    ("OVERRIDE_PACKAGE_VERIFICATION", "can bypass Play Protect / package verification"),
    ("QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES", "can enumerate every installed app"),
    ("BIND_ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICE", "can abuse Accessibility Service (screen reading, auto-click — common banking-trojan technique)"),
    ("SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW", "can draw over other apps (overlay/fake-login-screen attacks)"),
    ("BIND_DEVICE_ADMIN", "can request device admin (hard to uninstall, lock-screen control)"),
    ("READ_SMS", "can read SMS (OTP/2FA theft)"),
    ("RECEIVE_SMS", "can intercept incoming SMS (OTP/2FA theft)"),
    ("SEND_SMS", "can send SMS (premium-rate fraud, worm propagation)"),
    ("READ_CALL_LOG", "can harvest call history"),
    ("READ_CONTACTS", "can harvest contacts"),
    ("RECORD_AUDIO", "can record audio"),
    ("CAMERA", "can access the camera"),
];

/// The exact DN of AOSP's publicly known "testkey" (build/target/product/security/testkey.*
/// in the AOSP source tree). Genuine Google/OEM production apps are never signed with it —
/// seeing it on a device is close to definitive proof of a repackaged/tampered APK.
const AOSP_TESTKEY_MARKERS: &[&str] = &["CN=Android", "O=Android", "android@android.com"];

/// Sorted installed package names, or empty if no device / the call failed.
/// Shared by the log-streaming `list_packages` and the TUI's package picker.
pub fn packages() -> Vec<String> {
    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        return Vec::new();
    }
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["shell", "pm", "list", "packages"]);
    if !ok {
        return Vec::new();
    }
    // No name-based filtering here — the picker's type-to-filter search
    // handles narrowing the list, and hiding packages by name is exactly
    // the kind of thing a disguised/malicious package can exploit.
    let mut pkgs: Vec<String> =
        out.lines().filter_map(|l| l.strip_prefix("package:")).map(str::to_string).collect();
    pkgs.sort_unstable();
    pkgs
}

pub fn list_packages(tx: &Sender<String>) {
    tx.send("== Installed packages ==".into()).ok();
    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }

    tx.send("> pm list packages...".into()).ok();
    let pkgs = packages();
    if pkgs.is_empty() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] no packages found (or the adb call failed).".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    tx.send(format!("[OK] {} packages", pkgs.len())).ok();
    for p in pkgs {
        tx.send(format!("    {p}")).ok();
    }
}

/// Launches a package's launcher activity via `monkey`, which resolves it
/// automatically instead of needing to guess `pkg/.MainActivity`.
pub fn launch(pkg: &str, tx: &Sender<String>) {
    let pkg = pkg.trim();
    tx.send(format!("== Launching {pkg} ==")).ok();
    if pkg.is_empty() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No package name given.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }

    tx.send("> monkey -p <pkg> -c LAUNCHER...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["shell", "monkey", "-p", pkg, "-c", "android.intent.category.LAUNCHER", "1"]);
    tx.send(if ok && out.contains("Events injected: 1") {
        "[OK] launched".into()
    } else {
        format!("[FAIL] {out}")
    })
    .ok();
}

/// Pulls everything `dumpsys package <pkg>` exposes and presents it as a
/// curated report (identity, paths, flags, permissions, user state), with
/// the full raw dump appended so nothing extractable is left out.
pub fn package_info(pkg: &str, tx: &Sender<String>) {
    let pkg = pkg.trim();
    tx.send(format!("== Package info: {pkg} ==")).ok();

    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }

    tx.send("> dumpsys package...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["shell", "dumpsys", "package", pkg]);
    if !ok || out.trim().is_empty() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] no info returned — check the package name.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    let lines: Vec<&str> = out.lines().collect();

    let field = |marker: &str| -> Option<String> {
        lines.iter().find(|l| l.trim_start().starts_with(marker)).map(|l| l.trim().to_string())
    };
    let section = |tx: &Sender<String>, title: &str, markers: &[&str]| {
        let found: Vec<String> = markers.iter().filter_map(|m| field(m)).collect();
        if found.is_empty() {
            return;
        }
        tx.send(String::new()).ok();
        tx.send(format!("-- {title} --")).ok();
        for line in found {
            tx.send(format!("  {line}")).ok();
        }
    };

    tx.send("[OK] report:".into()).ok();

    // Pull the APK once (if possible) and reuse it for both the manifest
    // dump and the signature check, instead of pulling it twice.
    tx.send("> pulling APK for manifest/signature checks...".into()).ok();
    let apk_path = pull_apk_to(pkg, &cache::dir(), tx);
    let manifest = apk_path.as_ref().and_then(|p| manifest_info(p));
    let cert = apk_path.as_ref().and_then(|p| signing_cert(p));
    if let Some(p) = &apk_path {
        let _ = std::fs::remove_file(p); // don't leave pulled APKs lying around in the cache
    }

    tx.send(String::new()).ok();
    tx.send(format!("Package: {pkg}")).ok();
    tx.send(format!("App label: {}", manifest.as_ref().map(|m| m.label.as_str()).unwrap_or("(unknown — aapt unavailable or pull failed)"))).ok();

    let requested = indented_block_after(&lines, "requested permissions:");
    let code_path = field("codePath=").unwrap_or_default();
    let looks_like_system_name = pkg.starts_with("com.google") || pkg.starts_with("com.android") || pkg.starts_with("android.");
    let sideloaded = !code_path.contains("/system") && !code_path.contains("/product") && !code_path.contains("/vendor") && !code_path.contains("/apex");

    let mut flags: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    if looks_like_system_name && sideloaded {
        flags.push(format!(
            "Package name '{pkg}' impersonates a system/Google component{}, but is installed as a \
             regular sideloaded app ({}). Real system packages live in /system, /product, or /vendor — \
             never /data/app.",
            manifest.as_ref().map(|m| format!(" (app label: \"{}\")", m.label)).unwrap_or_default(),
            if code_path.is_empty() { "codePath unknown" } else { &code_path }
        ));
    }
    let version_code = field("versionCode=").and_then(|l| l.split_whitespace().next()?.strip_prefix("versionCode=").map(str::to_string));
    let version_name = field("versionName=").and_then(|l| l.strip_prefix("versionName=").map(|v| v.trim().to_string()));
    if version_code.as_deref() == Some("1") || version_name.as_deref() == Some("1.0") {
        flags.push("Placeholder version (versionCode=1 / versionName=1.0) — unusual for any app with real update history.".into());
    }
    if field("flags=[").map(|l| l.contains("DEBUGGABLE")).unwrap_or(false) {
        flags.push("Marked DEBUGGABLE — unusual for a release build; expands attack surface (can be attached to, run as any user).".into());
    }
    for (perm, reason) in SENSITIVE_PERMISSIONS {
        if requested.iter().any(|p| p.contains(perm)) {
            flags.push(format!("Requests {perm} — {reason}."));
        }
    }
    if let Some((dn, _)) = &cert {
        if AOSP_TESTKEY_MARKERS.iter().all(|m| dn.contains(m)) {
            flags.push(
                "Signed with AOSP's publicly known \"testkey\" (build/target/product/security/testkey.* \
                 in the AOSP source) — legitimate Google/OEM production apps are NEVER signed with this \
                 key. Near-definitive evidence of a repackaged or tampered APK."
                    .into(),
            );
        }
    }

    if !flags.is_empty() {
        tx.send(String::new()).ok();
        tx.send(format!("-- !! RED FLAGS ({}) !! --", flags.len())).ok();
        for f in &flags {
            tx.send(format!("  ! {f}")).ok();
        }
    }

    if let Some(m) = &manifest {
        tx.send(String::new()).ok();
        tx.send("-- Manifest info (aapt) --".into()).ok();
        tx.send(format!("  label: {}", m.label)).ok();
        if let Some(a) = &m.launchable_activity {
            tx.send(format!("  launchable-activity: {a}")).ok();
        }
        if let Some(n) = &m.native_code {
            tx.send(format!("  native-code: {n}")).ok();
        }
        if let Some(s) = &m.sdk_versions {
            tx.send(format!("  {s}")).ok();
        }
    }

    if let Some((dn, sha256)) = &cert {
        tx.send(String::new()).ok();
        tx.send("-- Signing certificate --".into()).ok();
        tx.send(format!("  DN: {dn}")).ok();
        tx.send(format!("  SHA-256: {sha256}")).ok();
    } else if apk_path.is_some() {
        tx.send(String::new()).ok();
        tx.send("[!] Couldn't read a signing certificate (apksigner missing, or the APK is unsigned/corrupt — itself worth noting).".into()).ok();
    }

    section(tx, "Identity", &["versionCode=", "versionName=", "firstInstallTime=", "lastUpdateTime=", "installerPackageName="]);
    section(tx, "Install paths", &["codePath=", "resourcePath=", "dataDir=", "primaryCpuAbi=", "secondaryCpuAbi="]);
    section(tx, "Flags", &["flags=[", "privateFlags=[", "pkgFlags=["]);
    section(tx, "User state", &["installed=", "hidden=", "suspended=", "stopped=", "enabled="]);

    if !requested.is_empty() {
        tx.send(String::new()).ok();
        tx.send(format!("-- Requested permissions ({}) --", requested.len())).ok();
        for p in &requested {
            tx.send(format!("  {p}")).ok();
        }
    }
    let runtime = indented_block_after(&lines, "runtime permissions:");
    if !runtime.is_empty() {
        tx.send(String::new()).ok();
        tx.send(format!("-- Runtime permissions granted ({}) --", runtime.len())).ok();
        for p in runtime {
            tx.send(format!("  {p}")).ok();
        }
    }

    if let Some(m) = &manifest {
        tx.send(String::new()).ok();
        tx.send(format!("-- Raw aapt dump badging output ({} lines) --", m.raw.len())).ok();
        for l in &m.raw {
            tx.send(l.clone()).ok();
        }
    }

    tx.send(String::new()).ok();
    tx.send(format!("-- Raw dumpsys output ({} lines) --", lines.len())).ok();
    for l in lines {
        tx.send(l.to_string()).ok();
    }
}

/// Pulls an installed package's APK into `dest_dir`. None if the device/pull/pm
/// path lookup fails. Shared by package info (signature/manifest checks),
/// apktool, and JADX — each decides for itself whether to keep the file after.
pub fn pull_apk_to(pkg: &str, dest_dir: &std::path::Path, tx: &Sender<String>) -> Option<std::path::PathBuf> {
    let (ok, path_out) = shell::adb_run(&["shell", "pm", "path", pkg]);
    if !ok {
        return None;
    }
    let device_path = path_out.lines().find_map(|l| l.strip_prefix("package:"))?.trim();

    let _ = std::fs::create_dir_all(dest_dir);
    let local = dest_dir.join(format!("{pkg}.apk"));
    let local_str = local.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    let (ok, _) = shell::adb_run(&["pull", device_path, &local_str]);
    if !ok {
        tx.send("[!] couldn't pull the APK.".into()).ok();
        return None;
    }
    Some(local)
}

struct ManifestInfo {
    label: String,
    launchable_activity: Option<String>,
    native_code: Option<String>,
    sdk_versions: Option<String>,
    raw: Vec<String>,
}

/// Runs `aapt dump badging` on a local APK copy for the app's real label
/// (what the user actually sees, as opposed to the package ID) plus a few
/// other manifest-derived facts the installed-package dump doesn't carry.
fn manifest_info(apk_path: &std::path::Path) -> Option<ManifestInfo> {
    let aapt = shell::locate("aapt", "version", sdk::aapt_path())?;
    let apk_str = apk_path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    let (ok, out) = shell::run(&aapt, &["dump", "badging", &apk_str]);
    if !ok {
        return None;
    }
    let raw: Vec<String> = out.lines().map(str::to_string).collect();

    let label = raw
        .iter()
        .find_map(|l| l.strip_prefix("application-label:"))
        .or_else(|| raw.iter().find_map(|l| l.split_once("application-label-en:").map(|(_, v)| v)))
        .map(|v| v.trim().trim_matches('\'').to_string())
        .unwrap_or_else(|| "(none set)".to_string());

    let launchable_activity =
        raw.iter().find_map(|l| l.strip_prefix("launchable-activity:")).map(|v| v.trim().to_string());
    let native_code = raw.iter().find_map(|l| l.strip_prefix("native-code:")).map(|v| v.trim().to_string());
    let sdk_lines: Vec<&str> = raw
        .iter()
        .filter(|l| l.starts_with("sdkVersion:") || l.starts_with("targetSdkVersion:"))
        .map(String::as_str)
        .collect();
    let sdk_versions = (!sdk_lines.is_empty()).then(|| sdk_lines.join(" "));

    Some(ManifestInfo { label, launchable_activity, native_code, sdk_versions, raw })
}

/// Runs `apksigner` against a local APK copy — a package name or permission
/// set can lie, but the signer is cryptographic. (DN, SHA-256), or None.
fn signing_cert(apk_path: &std::path::Path) -> Option<(String, String)> {
    let apksigner = shell::locate("apksigner", "--version", sdk::apksigner_path())?;
    let apk_str = apk_path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    let (ok, out) = shell::run(&apksigner, &["verify", "--print-certs", &apk_str]);
    if !ok {
        return None;
    }
    let dn = out.lines().find_map(|l| l.split_once("certificate DN: ").map(|(_, v)| v.trim().to_string()))?;
    let sha256 =
        out.lines().find_map(|l| l.split_once("SHA-256 digest: ").map(|(_, v)| v.trim().to_string())).unwrap_or_default();
    Some((dn, sha256))
}

/// Collects the lines more-indented than `header` immediately following it —
/// e.g. the permission list under "requested permissions:".
fn indented_block_after(lines: &[&str], header: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    let Some(idx) = lines.iter().position(|l| l.trim() == header) else {
        return Vec::new();
    };
    let header_indent = lines[idx].len() - lines[idx].trim_start().len();
    lines[idx + 1..]
        .iter()
        .take_while(|l| !l.trim().is_empty() && l.len() - l.trim_start().len() > header_indent)
        .map(|l| l.trim().to_string())
        .collect()
}

pub fn install_apk(path: &str, tx: &Sender<String>) {
    // Windows' "Copy as path" wraps the result in "quotes" — strip them.
    let path = path.trim().trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'').trim();
    tx.send(format!("== Installing {path} ==")).ok();
    if path.is_empty() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No APK path given.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }

    tx.send("> adb install -r...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["install", "-r", path]);
    tx.send(if ok { "[OK] installed".into() } else { format!("[FAIL] {out}") }).ok();
}

pub fn uninstall(pkg: &str, tx: &Sender<String>) {
    let pkg = pkg.trim();
    tx.send(format!("== Uninstalling {pkg} ==")).ok();
    if pkg.is_empty() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No package name given.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }

    tx.send("> adb uninstall...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["uninstall", pkg]);
    tx.send(if ok { "[OK] uninstalled".into() } else { format!("[FAIL] {out}") }).ok();
}

pub fn disable(pkg: &str, tx: &Sender<String>) {
    let pkg = pkg.trim();
    tx.send(format!("== Disabling {pkg} ==")).ok();
    if pkg.is_empty() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No package name given.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }

    tx.send("> pm disable-user...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["shell", "pm", "disable-user", "--user", "0", pkg]);
    tx.send(if ok { format!("[OK] {out}") } else { format!("[FAIL] {out}") }).ok();
}

pub fn enable(pkg: &str, tx: &Sender<String>) {
    let pkg = pkg.trim();
    tx.send(format!("== Enabling {pkg} ==")).ok();
    if pkg.is_empty() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No package name given.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }

    tx.send("> pm enable...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["shell", "pm", "enable", pkg]);
    tx.send(if ok { format!("[OK] {out}") } else { format!("[FAIL] {out}") }).ok();
}
```

`src/modules/emulator.rs`:

```rs
use std::sync::mpsc::Sender;

use crate::util::{sdk, shell};

pub const DEFAULT_AVD: &str = "pentest-avd";
/// Google APIs, no Play Store: the one combination that's both rootable
/// (adb root works) and has the broadest package/API compatibility.
const SYSTEM_IMAGE: &str = "system-images;android-33;google_apis;x86_64";

pub fn detect(tx: &Sender<String>) {
    tx.send("== Emulator detection ==".into()).ok();

    match shell::locate("emulator", "-version", sdk::emulator_path()) {
        Some(bin) => {
            let (_, version) = shell::run(&bin, &["-version"]);
            let short_version = version.split_whitespace().nth(3).unwrap_or("?");
            tx.send(format!("[OK] emulator installed (v{short_version})")).ok();

            tx.send("> listing AVDs...".into()).ok();
            let names = list_avd_names(&bin);
            if names.is_empty() {
                tx.send("[!] No AVDs found. Use 'Create new AVD' below.".into()).ok();
            } else {
                tx.send("[OK] AVDs found:".into()).ok();
                for name in &names {
                    let rootable = if avd_is_rootable(name) { "rootable" } else { "Play Store, no root" };
                    let line = match avd_summary(name) {
                        Some(summary) => format!("    - {name}  ({summary}, {rootable})"),
                        None => format!("    - {name}  ({rootable})"),
                    };
                    tx.send(line).ok();
                }
            }
        }
        None => {
            tx.send("[MISSING] Android emulator not found.".into()).ok();
            match sdk::sdk_root() {
                Some(root) => {
                    tx.send(format!(
                        "  SDK found at {} but no emulator binary — install the 'Android Emulator' package via Android Studio's SDK Manager.",
                        root.display()
                    ))
                    .ok();
                }
                None => {
                    tx.send("  -> Install Android Studio: https://developer.android.com/studio".into()).ok();
                }
            }
        }
    }

    tx.send("> checking connected devices...".into()).ok();
    let devices = shell::adb_devices();
    let online: Vec<_> = devices.iter().filter(|(_, s)| s == "device").collect();
    match online.len() {
        0 => {
            tx.send("[!] No device online yet (adb devices shows none, or it's still booting/offline).".into())
                .ok();
        }
        1 => {
            tx.send(format!("[OK] device online: {}", online[0].0)).ok();
        }
        _ => {
            tx.send(format!("[OK] {} devices online.", online.len())).ok();
            tx.send("[!] Multiple devices attached — use 'Select ADB device' (main menu) to pick which one actions target.".into()).ok();
        }
    }
}

/// Returns true if the AVD ends up created.
pub fn create_avd(tx: &Sender<String>) -> bool {
    tx.send(format!("== Creating AVD '{DEFAULT_AVD}' ==")).ok();

    let Some(sdkmanager) = shell::locate("sdkmanager", "--version", sdk::sdkmanager_path()) else {
        tx.send("[MISSING] sdkmanager not found — install Android Studio's cmdline-tools first.".into())
            .ok();
        return false;
    };
    // avdmanager has no --version flag (exits 1 with a usage error on it);
    // `list` is a harmless no-op that reliably exits 0 when it's working.
    let Some(avdmanager) = shell::locate("avdmanager", "list", sdk::avdmanager_path()) else {
        tx.send("[MISSING] avdmanager not found — install Android Studio's cmdline-tools first.".into())
            .ok();
        return false;
    };

    tx.send(format!("> installing system image {SYSTEM_IMAGE} (this can take a while)...")).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::run_streaming(&sdkmanager, &[SYSTEM_IMAGE], tx);
    if !ok {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {out}")).ok();
        return false;
    }
    tx.send("[OK] system image ready".into()).ok();

    tx.send("> creating AVD...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::run(
        &avdmanager,
        &[
            "create", "avd", "-n", DEFAULT_AVD, "-k", SYSTEM_IMAGE, "-d", "pixel_6", "--force",
        ],
    );
    tx.send(if ok {
        format!("[OK] AVD '{DEFAULT_AVD}' created — Google APIs, no Play Store, fully rootable")
    } else {
        format!("[FAIL] {out}")
    })
    .ok();
    ok
}

fn list_avd_names(emulator_bin: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    let (_, avds) = shell::run(emulator_bin, &["-list-avds"]);
    avds.lines().map(str::trim).filter(|l| !l.is_empty()).map(str::to_string).collect()
}

/// An AVD built from a Play Store system image refuses `adb root`
/// unconditionally — not something this tool (or any tool) can work around.
pub fn avd_is_rootable(name: &str) -> bool {
    avd_config_path(name)
        .and_then(|p| std::fs::read_to_string(p).ok())
        .map(|contents| !contents.lines().any(|l| l.trim() == "PlayStore.enabled=true"))
        .unwrap_or(true) // unknown config shouldn't block the flow
}

/// Prefers an AVD known to support root; falls back to whatever exists.
pub fn preferred_avd_name() -> Option<String> {
    let bin = shell::locate("emulator", "-version", sdk::emulator_path())?;
    let names = list_avd_names(&bin);
    names.iter().find(|n| avd_is_rootable(n)).cloned().or_else(|| names.into_iter().next())
}

/// True if none of the existing AVDs support root (including "no AVDs at all").
pub fn needs_rootable_avd() -> bool {
    let Some(bin) = shell::locate("emulator", "-version", sdk::emulator_path()) else {
        return true;
    };
    !list_avd_names(&bin).iter().any(|n| avd_is_rootable(n))
}

/// Launches the preferred (rootable, if one exists) AVD out of whatever
/// actually exists on this machine — never a hardcoded name, since a name
/// with no matching AVD just fails with a confusing "AVD not found" error.
pub fn launch_avd(tx: &Sender<String>) -> bool {
    let Some(bin) = shell::locate("emulator", "-version", sdk::emulator_path()) else {
        tx.send("[MISSING] Android emulator not found.".into()).ok();
        return false;
    };
    let Some(name) = preferred_avd_name() else {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No AVDs found. Use 'Create new AVD' first.".into()).ok();
        return false;
    };
    tx.send(format!("== Launching '{name}' ==")).ok();

    tx.send("> clearing stale adb device state...".into()).ok();
    shell::adb_run(&["kill-server"]);

    match shell::spawn_bg(&bin, &["-avd", &name, "-writable-system"]) {
        Ok(()) => {
            tx.send(format!("[OK] '{name}' launching in the background (boot takes ~30-90s).")).ok();
            true
        }
        Err(e) => {
            tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {e}")).ok();
            false
        }
    }
}

/// Restarts adbd as root (`adb root`). Only works on non-Play-Store/"userdebug"
/// system images — production (Play Store) builds refuse this unconditionally.
pub fn root_device(tx: &Sender<String>) {
    tx.send("== adb root ==".into()).ok();
    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["root"]);
    let rooted = ok && !out.contains("cannot run as root");
    if rooted {
        tx.send(format!("[OK] {out}")).ok();
    } else {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {out}")).ok();
        tx.send("This device's image doesn't support root (it's a Play Store system image —".into()).ok();
        tx.send("adb root is disabled unconditionally on these, no workaround exists).".into()).ok();
        tx.send("Run 'Create new AVD': it builds a Google APIs image (no Play Store, Android 13,".into()).ok();
        tx.send("x86_64) that's rootable and has the widest package/API compatibility for testing.".into())
            .ok();
    }
}

/// Restarts adbd back to unprivileged mode (`adb unroot`).
pub fn deroot_device(tx: &Sender<String>) {
    tx.send("== adb unroot ==".into()).ok();
    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["unroot"]);
    tx.send(if ok { format!("[OK] {out}") } else { format!("[FAIL] {out}") }).ok();
}

/// Polls `sys.boot_completed` until the device finishes booting or times out.
pub fn wait_for_boot(tx: &Sender<String>, timeout_secs: u64) -> bool {
    tx.send(format!("> waiting up to {timeout_secs}s for the device to finish booting...")).ok();
    let start = std::time::Instant::now();
    let mut last_heartbeat = 0u64;
    while start.elapsed().as_secs() < timeout_secs {
        let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["shell", "getprop", "sys.boot_completed"]);
        if ok && out.trim() == "1" {
            tx.send("[OK] device finished booting.".into()).ok();
            return true;
        }
        let elapsed = start.elapsed().as_secs();
        if elapsed / 15 > last_heartbeat / 15 {
            tx.send(format!("> still booting... ({elapsed}s)")).ok();
        }
        last_heartbeat = elapsed;
        std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(3));
    }
    tx.send(format!("[FAIL] device didn't finish booting within {timeout_secs}s — check it manually.")).ok();
    false
}

/// Condenses config.ini into one readable fragment, e.g. "Android 37.0, x86_64, 2816MB RAM".
fn avd_summary(name: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let contents = std::fs::read_to_string(avd_config_path(name)?).ok()?;
    let value_of = |key: &str| -> Option<String> {
        contents
            .lines()
            .find(|l| l.starts_with(key))
            .and_then(|l| l.split_once('='))
            .map(|(_, v)| v.trim().to_string())
    };

    let abi = value_of("abi.type").unwrap_or_else(|| "?".into());
    // Some AVDs store a bare MB number ("2816"), others a value+unit ("2G") — only append "MB" to the former.
    let ram = value_of("hw.ramSize")
        .map(|r| if r.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_digit()) { format!("{r}MB RAM") } else { format!("{r} RAM") })
        .unwrap_or_else(|| "? RAM".into());
    let api = value_of("image.sysdir.1")
        .and_then(|sysdir| {
            sysdir
                .split(['\\', '/'])
                .find(|part| part.starts_with("android-"))
                .map(|part| part.trim_start_matches("android-").to_string())
        })
        .unwrap_or_else(|| "?".into());

    Some(format!("Android {api}, {abi}, {ram}"))
}

/// AVDs live under ~/.android/avd regardless of where the SDK itself is installed.
fn avd_config_path(name: &str) -> Option<std::path::PathBuf> {
    let home = std::env::var("USERPROFILE").or_else(|_| std::env::var("HOME")).ok()?;
    Some(
        std::path::Path::new(&home)
            .join(".android")
            .join("avd")
            .join(format!("{name}.avd"))
            .join("config.ini"),
    )
}
```

`src/modules/jadx.rs`:

```rs
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::sync::mpsc::Sender;

use super::apps;
use crate::util::{cache, github, shell};

const REPO: &str = "skylot/jadx";

fn install_dir() -> PathBuf {
    cache::dir().join("jadx")
}

fn gui_bin() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    let candidate = install_dir().join("bin").join("jadx-gui.bat");
    candidate.exists().then_some(candidate)
}

/// Ensures JADX is extracted locally, downloading the latest release if not.
/// Returns the path to `jadx-gui.bat` on success.
pub fn install(tx: &Sender<String>) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    if let Some(bin) = gui_bin() {
        return Some(bin);
    }

    tx.send("== Installing JADX ==".into()).ok();
    if !shell::is_installed("java", "-version") {
        tx.send("[MISSING] Java not found — JADX needs a JRE. Install Java first.".into()).ok();
        return None;
    }

    tx.send("> checking latest JADX release...".into()).ok();
    let Some(body) = github::fetch_release_json(REPO) else {
        tx.send("[FAIL] couldn't reach GitHub to find the latest JADX release.".into()).ok();
        return None;
    };
    let Some(tag) = github::json_string(&body, "tag_name") else {
        tx.send("[FAIL] couldn't parse the latest JADX release.".into()).ok();
        return None;
    };
    let version = tag.trim_start_matches('v');
    // The release also ships GUI-only platform builds (jadx-gui-<v>-win.zip,
    // *.dmg) — match the exact full-bundle name so we don't grab one of those.
    let exact_name = format!("jadx-{version}.zip");
    let Some(url) = github::json_string_matching(&body, "browser_download_url", &exact_name) else {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] couldn't find a {exact_name} asset in the latest release.")).ok();
        return None;
    };

    let zip_path = cache::dir().join(&exact_name);
    let zip_str = zip_path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    tx.send(format!("> downloading {exact_name}...")).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::run_streaming("curl", &["-L", "-o", &zip_str, &url], tx);
    if !ok {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {out}")).ok();
        return None;
    }

    let dir = install_dir();
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    let _ = std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir);
    tx.send("> extracting...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::run("tar", &["-xf", &zip_str, "-C", &dir.to_string_lossy()]);
    let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&zip_path);
    if !ok {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] extract failed: {out}")).ok();
        return None;
    }

    let bin = dir.join("bin").join("jadx-gui.bat");
    if !bin.exists() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] extracted, but jadx-gui.bat wasn't where expected.".into()).ok();
        return None;
    }
    tx.send(format!("[OK] JADX {version} ready")).ok();
    Some(bin)
}

/// Picks a package from the device, pulls its APK into `workspace/jadx/<pkg>/`,
/// and opens it directly in jadx-gui.
pub fn open_package(pkg: &str, tx: &Sender<String>) {
    let pkg = pkg.trim();
    tx.send(format!("== Opening {pkg} in JADX ==")).ok();

    let Some(gui) = install(tx) else { return };
    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }

    let dest_dir = Path::new("workspace").join("jadx").join(pkg);
    tx.send("> pulling APK from device...".into()).ok();
    let Some(apk) = apps::pull_apk_to(pkg, &dest_dir, tx) else { return };
    tx.send(format!("[OK] pulled to {}", apk.display())).ok();

    tx.send("> launching jadx-gui...".into()).ok();
    match shell::spawn_bg(&gui.to_string_lossy(), &[&apk.to_string_lossy()]) {
        Ok(()) => {
            tx.send("[OK] jadx-gui launched.".into()).ok();
        }
        Err(e) => {
            tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {e}")).ok();
        }
    }
}
```

`src/modules/mitm.rs`:

```rs
use std::sync::mpsc::Sender;

use super::emulator;
use crate::util::{sdk, shell};

pub const PROXY_PORT: u16 = 8080;
pub const WEB_PORT: u16 = 8081;

/// Android connectivity-check / Play-services telemetry hosts that aren't
/// useful noise in a traffic-analysis session — fully bypassed via
/// --ignore-hosts (plain TCP passthrough, not just hidden from the UI).
const IGNORED_HOSTS: &[&str] = &[
    r"connectivitycheck\.gstatic\.com",
    r"connectivitycheck\.android\.com",
    r"play\.googleapis\.com",
];

/// Returns true if mitmweb ends up installed.
pub fn install(tx: &Sender<String>) -> bool {
    tx.send("== mitmproxy install ==".into()).ok();
    if shell::is_installed("mitmweb", "--version") {
        let (_, v) = shell::run("mitmweb", &["--version"]);
        tx.send(format!("[OK] {}", v.lines().next().unwrap_or(&v))).ok();
        true
    } else {
        tx.send("[MISSING] mitmproxy not found.".into()).ok();
        tx.send("> pip install mitmproxy...".into()).ok();
        let (ok, out) = shell::run_streaming("pip", &["install", "mitmproxy"], tx);
        tx.send(if ok {
            "[OK] mitmproxy installed".into()
        } else {
            format!("[FAIL] {out}")
        })
        .ok();
        ok
    }
}

/// Starts mitmweb (proxy + browser UI) in the background and points the connected
/// emulator's global HTTP proxy at the host (10.0.2.2 is the host, from the emulator's view).
/// Returns true if mitmweb started; the device-proxy step is a soft warning, not a blocker.
pub fn start_and_set_proxy(tx: &Sender<String>) -> bool {
    tx.send("== Starting mitmweb & pointing device at it ==".into()).ok();

    // mitmweb normally opens the browser itself with its auto-generated token
    // baked into the URL, but when we pin web_password ourselves (so we can
    // know and print the URL), its own web_url omits the token — so we set a
    // known token, suppress mitmweb's launch, and open the browser ourselves.
    let token = shell::random_token();
    let mut args = vec![
        "--listen-host".to_string(),
        "0.0.0.0".to_string(),
        "--listen-port".to_string(),
        PROXY_PORT.to_string(),
        "--web-port".to_string(),
        WEB_PORT.to_string(),
        "--no-web-open-browser".to_string(),
        "--set".to_string(),
        format!("web_password={token}"),
    ];
    for host in IGNORED_HOSTS {
        args.push("--ignore-hosts".to_string());
        args.push(host.to_string());
    }
    let args: Vec<&str> = args.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();

    tx.send("> starting mitmweb...".into()).ok();
    match shell::spawn_bg("mitmweb", &args) {
        Ok(()) => {
            tx.send(format!("[OK] mitmweb listening on 0.0.0.0:{PROXY_PORT}")).ok();
            tx.send("  (connectivity-check/Play-services noise bypassed, won't show as flows)".into()).ok();
        }
        Err(e) => {
            tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {e}")).ok();
            return false;
        }
    }

    std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(800));
    let ui_url = format!("http://127.0.0.1:{WEB_PORT}/?token={token}");
    tx.send(format!("> opening {ui_url} ...")).ok();
    match shell::open_url(&ui_url) {
        Ok(()) => {
            tx.send("[OK] browser opened".into()).ok();
        }
        Err(e) => {
            tx.send(format!("[!] couldn't auto-open a browser ({e}) — open it manually:")).ok();
            tx.send(format!("    {ui_url}")).ok();
        }
    }

    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[!] No device connected — once it's up, set its proxy manually:".into()).ok();
        tx.send(format!("    adb shell settings put global http_proxy 10.0.2.2:{PROXY_PORT}")).ok();
        return true;
    }

    tx.send("> setting device proxy...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) =
        shell::adb_run(&["shell", &format!("settings put global http_proxy 10.0.2.2:{PROXY_PORT}")]);
    tx.send(if ok {
        format!("[OK] device proxy set to 10.0.2.2:{PROXY_PORT}")
    } else {
        format!("[FAIL] {out}")
    })
    .ok();
    true
}

/// Looks for mitmproxy's generated CA cert, hashes it the way Android's system
/// trust store expects, pushes it as a system CA, and reboots the device.
/// Returns true only if the cert ends up installed and the reboot was issued.
pub fn install_cert(tx: &Sender<String>) -> bool {
    tx.send("== Install mitmproxy CA cert on device ==".into()).ok();

    // adb root restarts adbd (disconnect+reconnect) partway through this
    // sequence. Pin one serial for the whole thing so a mid-sequence device
    // list hiccup can't make remount/push silently land on a different
    // device than the one that was just rooted.
    let previous_override = shell::selected_device();
    let pinned = shell::adb_target();
    if let Some(serial) = &pinned {
        shell::set_selected_device(Some(serial.clone()));
    }
    let result = install_cert_pinned(tx, pinned.as_deref());
    shell::set_selected_device(previous_override);
    result
}

fn install_cert_pinned(tx: &Sender<String>, serial: Option<&str>) -> bool {
    let cert_path = cert_source_path();
    if !cert_path.exists() {
        tx.send(format!("[!] {} not found yet.", cert_path.display())).ok();
        tx.send("    Start the proxy, point the device at it, then visit any http://".into()).ok();
        tx.send("    site on the device so mitmproxy generates its CA cert, then retry.".into()).ok();
        return false;
    }

    let Some(openssl) = shell::locate("openssl", "version", sdk::git_for_windows_tool("openssl")) else {
        tx.send("[MISSING] openssl not found, can't compute the Android cert filename.".into()).ok();
        return false;
    };

    tx.send("> hashing cert...".into()).ok();
    let cert_str = cert_path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    let (ok, hash_out) = shell::run(
        &openssl,
        &["x509", "-inform", "PEM", "-subject_hash_old", "-in", &cert_str],
    );
    let hash = hash_out.lines().next().unwrap_or("").trim().to_string();
    if !ok || hash.is_empty() {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] could not hash cert: {hash_out}")).ok();
        return false;
    }
    tx.send(format!("[OK] cert hash: {hash}")).ok();

    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device connected.".into()).ok();
        return false;
    }

    let tmp_named = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("{hash}.0"));
    if std::fs::copy(&cert_path, &tmp_named).is_err() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] could not stage renamed cert file.".into()).ok();
        return false;
    }
    let tmp_str = tmp_named.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    let dest = format!("/system/etc/security/cacerts/{hash}.0");

    let Some(remount_out) = root_and_remount(tx, serial) else {
        return false;
    };

    // On dm-verity-enabled images, `remount` can't make /system writable in
    // place — it just disables verity and demands a reboot before a second
    // remount will actually succeed. Do that reboot-and-retry cycle here
    // instead of pushing straight into what's still a read-only filesystem.
    if remount_out.contains("Now reboot") || remount_out.contains("Disabling verity") {
        tx.send("> verity needs a reboot before /system is actually writable — rebooting...".into())
            .ok();
        if let Err(e) = shell::adb_spawn_bg(&["reboot"]) {
            tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {e}")).ok();
            return false;
        }
        std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(3)); // let it actually go down first
        if !emulator::wait_for_boot(tx, 120) {
            return false;
        }
        let Some(_) = root_and_remount(tx, serial) else {
            return false;
        };
    }

    tx.send("> pushing cert...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["push", &tmp_str, &dest]);
    if !ok {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {out}")).ok();
        return false;
    }
    tx.send(format!("[OK] pushed cert to {dest}")).ok();

    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["shell", &format!("chmod 644 {dest}")]);
    if !ok {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {out}")).ok();
        return false;
    }
    tx.send("[OK] permissions set".into()).ok();

    tx.send("> rebooting device to load the new system CA...".into()).ok();
    match shell::adb_spawn_bg(&["reboot"]) {
        Ok(()) => {
            tx.send("[OK] reboot issued — traffic should decrypt once it's back up.".into()).ok();
            true
        }
        Err(e) => {
            tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {e}")).ok();
            false
        }
    }
}

/// `adb root` (waiting for adbd's restart to settle) then `adb remount`.
/// Returns remount's raw output on success (the caller checks it for the
/// dm-verity "reboot needed" case), or None if either step failed.
fn root_and_remount(tx: &Sender<String>, serial: Option<&str>) -> Option<String> {
    tx.send("> adb root...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["root"]);
    let rooted = ok && !out.contains("cannot run as root");
    tx.send(if rooted { "[OK] adb root".into() } else { format!("[FAIL] adb root: {out}") }).ok();
    if !rooted {
        tx.send("This device's image doesn't support root (likely a Play Store system image).".into())
            .ok();
        tx.send("Use 'Create new AVD' for a non-Play-Store, rootable image instead.".into()).ok();
        return None;
    }

    // adb root just restarted adbd — give it a moment to actually reconnect
    // in root mode before issuing anything else, or remount can silently
    // race a device that's momentarily offline/reconnecting.
    if let Some(serial) = serial {
        tx.send("> waiting for adbd to reconnect as root...".into()).ok();
        if !shell::wait_for_device(serial, 15) {
            tx.send("[FAIL] device didn't come back online after adb root.".into()).ok();
            return None;
        }
    }

    tx.send("> adb remount...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["remount"]);
    tx.send(format!("{} adb remount: {out}", if ok { "[OK]" } else { "[FAIL]" })).ok();
    ok.then_some(out)
}

fn cert_source_path() -> std::path::PathBuf {
    let home = std::env::var("USERPROFILE").or_else(|_| std::env::var("HOME")).unwrap_or_else(|_| ".".into());
    std::path::Path::new(&home).join(".mitmproxy").join("mitmproxy-ca-cert.cer")
}
```

```rs
use std::sync::mpsc::Sender;

use crate::util::shell;

const DOWNLOAD_PAGE: &str = "https://mt-manager.en.uptodown.com/android/download";

/// MT Manager's real download is served from a JS/AJAX-driven endpoint on
/// uptodown rather than a stable, scrapable URL — opening the page for a
/// manual download is more honest than shipping a scraper that silently
/// breaks the moment their frontend changes.
pub fn install(tx: &Sender<String>) {
    tx.send("== MT Manager ==".into()).ok();
    tx.send(format!("> opening {DOWNLOAD_PAGE} ...")).ok();
    match shell::open_url(DOWNLOAD_PAGE) {
        Ok(()) => {
            tx.send("[OK] opened in your browser.".into()).ok();
            tx.send("  Download the APK, then use 'Install APK' (App manager) with that path.".into()).ok();
        }
        Err(e) => {
            tx.send(format!("[FAIL] couldn't open a browser: {e}")).ok();
            tx.send(format!("  Download it manually from: {DOWNLOAD_PAGE}")).ok();
        }
    }
}
```

`src/modules/ssl_pin.rs`:

```rs
use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::sync::mpsc::Sender;
use std::sync::{Mutex, OnceLock};

use crate::util::{cache, sdk, shell};

/// Packages this tool has started a frida bypass session for, and the PID of
/// that session. Best-effort: doesn't notice if frida exits on its own.
fn sessions() -> &'static Mutex<HashMap<String, u32>> {
    static SESSIONS: OnceLock<Mutex<HashMap<String, u32>>> = OnceLock::new();
    SESSIONS.get_or_init(|| Mutex::new(HashMap::new()))
}

/// Packages currently believed to have an active bypass session.
pub fn active_bypasses() -> Vec<String> {
    sessions().lock().unwrap().keys().cloned().collect()
}

pub fn is_bypassed(pkg: &str) -> bool {
    sessions().lock().unwrap().contains_key(pkg)
}

/// Returns true if both frida and objection end up installed.
pub fn install_tools(tx: &Sender<String>) -> bool {
    tx.send("== Install frida-tools & objection ==".into()).ok();

    let frida_ok = if shell::is_installed("frida", "--version") {
        let (_, v) = shell::run("frida", &["--version"]);
        tx.send(format!("[OK] frida {v}")).ok();
        true
    } else {
        tx.send("[MISSING] frida not found.".into()).ok();
        tx.send("> pip install frida-tools...".into()).ok();
        let (ok, out) = shell::run_streaming("pip", &["install", "frida-tools"], tx);
        tx.send(if ok {
            "[OK] frida-tools installed".into()
        } else {
            format!("[FAIL] {out}")
        })
        .ok();
        ok
    };

    let objection_ok = if shell::is_installed("objection", "version") {
        tx.send("[OK] objection installed".into()).ok();
        true
    } else {
        tx.send("[MISSING] objection not found.".into()).ok();
        tx.send("> pip install objection...".into()).ok();
        let (ok, out) = shell::run_streaming("pip", &["install", "objection"], tx);
        tx.send(if ok {
            "[OK] objection installed".into()
        } else {
            format!("[FAIL] {out}")
        })
        .ok();
        ok
    };

    frida_ok && objection_ok
}

/// Downloads the frida-server build matching the host's frida version and the
/// connected device's ABI, pushes it, and starts it on the device (needs root).
/// Returns true only if every step up to "starting on device" succeeded.
pub fn push_frida_server(tx: &Sender<String>) -> bool {
    tx.send("== Push & start frida-server ==".into()).ok();

    if !shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No device/emulator connected (adb devices).".into()).ok();
        return false;
    }
    if !shell::is_installed("frida", "--version") {
        tx.send("[FAIL] `frida` not installed — run 'Install frida-tools & objection' first.".into())
            .ok();
        return false;
    }
    if !shell::is_installed("curl", "--version") {
        tx.send("[MISSING] `curl` not on PATH, can't auto-download frida-server.".into()).ok();
        tx.send("  -> Get it manually: https://github.com/frida/frida/releases".into()).ok();
        return false;
    }

    let (_, version) = shell::run("frida", &["--version"]);
    let version = version.trim().to_string();
    tx.send("> reading device ABI...".into()).ok();
    let (_, abi_raw) = shell::adb_run(&["shell", "getprop", "ro.product.cpu.abi"]);
    let abi_raw = abi_raw.trim();
    let abi = map_abi(abi_raw);

    tx.send(format!("Device ABI: {abi_raw} -> frida ABI: {abi}")).ok();
    tx.send(format!("Host frida version: {version}")).ok();

    let bin_name = format!("frida-server-{version}-android-{abi}");
    let cached_bin = cache::dir().join(&bin_name);

    if cached_bin.exists() {
        tx.send(format!("[OK] using cached frida-server ({})", cached_bin.display())).ok();
    } else {
        let fname = format!("{bin_name}.xz");
        let url = format!("https://github.com/frida/frida/releases/download/{version}/{fname}");
        let archive = cache::dir().join(&fname);
        let archive_str = archive.to_string_lossy().to_string();

        tx.send(format!("> downloading {fname}...")).ok();
        let (ok, out) = shell::run_streaming("curl", &["-L", "-o", &archive_str, &url], tx);
        if !ok {
            tx.send(format!("[FAIL] download failed: {out}")).ok();
            return false;
        }
        tx.send("[OK] downloaded".into()).ok();

        tx.send("> decompressing...".into()).ok();
        let decompressor = shell::locate("xz", "--version", sdk::git_for_windows_tool("xz"))
            .or_else(|| shell::locate("unxz", "--version", sdk::git_for_windows_tool("unxz")));
        let Some(decompressor) = decompressor else {
            tx.send("[MISSING] `xz`/`unxz` not found. Install Git for Windows (bundles xz) or 7-Zip.".into())
                .ok();
            return false;
        };
        let (ok, out) = shell::run(&decompressor, &["-d", "-f", &archive_str]);
        if !ok {
            tx.send(format!("[FAIL] decompress failed: {out}")).ok();
            return false;
        }
        tx.send(format!("[OK] cached at {}", cached_bin.display())).ok();
    }

    let bin_str = cached_bin.to_string_lossy().to_string();

    tx.send("> pushing to device...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["push", &bin_str, "/data/local/tmp/frida-server"]);
    if !ok {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {out}")).ok();
        return false;
    }
    tx.send("[OK] pushed to /data/local/tmp/frida-server".into()).ok();

    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["shell", "chmod 755 /data/local/tmp/frida-server"]);
    if !ok {
        tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {out}")).ok();
        return false;
    }
    tx.send("[OK] made executable".into()).ok();

    match shell::adb_spawn_bg(&["shell", "/data/local/tmp/frida-server &"]) {
        Ok(()) => {
            tx.send("[OK] frida-server starting on device (requires root).".into()).ok();
            true
        }
        Err(e) => {
            tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {e}")).ok();
            false
        }
    }
}

/// Spawns Frida against `package` with the universal SSL-pinning bypass script.
pub fn bypass(package: &str, tx: &Sender<String>) {
    let package = package.trim();
    tx.send(format!("== Bypassing pinning for '{package}' ==")).ok();

    if package.is_empty() {
        tx.send("[FAIL] No package name given.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    if !shell::is_installed("frida", "--version") {
        tx.send("[FAIL] frida not installed — run 'Install frida-tools & objection' first.".into())
            .ok();
        return;
    }

    // Target the specific device by serial: frida's bare -U picks arbitrarily
    // when more than one device/emulator is attached, which can silently hang
    // spawning on a device that has no frida-server running.
    let serial = shell::adb_target();
    let mut args: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
    match &serial {
        Some(s) => args.extend(["-D", s]),
        None => args.push("-U"),
    }
    args.extend(["-f", package, "-c", "akabe1/frida-multiple-unpinning"]);

    tx.send("> spawning target via Frida codeshare (akabe1/frida-multiple-unpinning)...".into()).ok();
    match shell::spawn_child("frida", &args) {
        Ok(child) => {
            sessions().lock().unwrap().insert(package.to_string(), child.id());
            tx.send("[OK] launched in the background — check the device/app.".into()).ok();
            tx.send("  (First run fetches the codeshare script; needs internet once.)".into()).ok();
            tx.send("  Requires a rooted device/AVD — frida-server can't spawn without root.".into()).ok();
        }
        Err(e) => {
            tx.send(format!("[FAIL] {e}")).ok();
        }
    }
}

/// Kills the tracked frida session and force-stops the app so its next launch
/// is clean (no injected agent) — i.e. pinning is back in effect.
pub fn stop_bypass(package: &str, tx: &Sender<String>) {
    let package = package.trim();
    tx.send(format!("== Stopping bypass for '{package}' (re-pinning) ==")).ok();

    let pid = sessions().lock().unwrap().remove(package);
    match pid {
        Some(pid) => {
            let (ok, out) = shell::kill_pid(pid);
            tx.send(if ok {
                "[OK] frida session stopped".into()
            } else {
                format!("[FAIL] couldn't kill frida (pid {pid}): {out}")
            })
            .ok();
        }
        None => {
            tx.send("[!] No bypass session tracked for this package by this tool.".into()).ok();
        }
    }

    tx.send("> force-stopping the app so its next launch is clean...".into()).ok();
    let (ok, out) = shell::adb_run(&["shell", "am", "force-stop", package]);
    tx.send(if ok { "[OK] app stopped".into() } else { format!("[FAIL] {out}") }).ok();
}

fn map_abi(abi: &str) -> &'static str {
    match abi {
        "x86_64" => "x86_64",
        "x86" => "x86",
        "arm64-v8a" => "arm64",
        _ => "arm",
    }
}
```

`src/modules/template.rs`:

```rs
use std::sync::mpsc::Sender;

use super::{emulator, mitm, ssl_pin};
use crate::util::shell;

/// End-to-end: boot (or create+boot) an AVD, wait for it, install SSL-pinning
/// tooling and push frida-server, then stand up mitmweb and trust its CA.
/// Stops at the first failed step instead of continuing past it.
pub fn run(tx: &Sender<String>) {
    tx.send("== Full setup: emulator, SSL tooling, mitmproxy sandbox ==".into()).ok();

    emulator::detect(tx);

    if shell::adb_device_connected() {
        tx.send("[OK] device already online — skipping boot.".into()).ok();
    } else {
        if emulator::needs_rootable_avd() {
            tx.send(
                "No rootable AVD found (either none exist, or the only ones are Play Store \
                 images) — the cert-install and frida steps both need root, so creating one."
                    .into(),
            )
            .ok();
            if !emulator::create_avd(tx) {
                tx.send("Stopped: couldn't create an AVD. Fix the error above, then re-run.".into()).ok();
                return;
            }
        }
        if !emulator::launch_avd(tx) {
            tx.send("Stopped: couldn't launch the emulator. Fix the error above, then re-run.".into())
                .ok();
            return;
        }
        if !emulator::wait_for_boot(tx, 150) {
            tx.send("Stopped: emulator didn't finish booting. Fix the issue, then re-run.".into()).ok();
            return;
        }
    }

    if !ssl_pin::install_tools(tx) {
        tx.send("Stopped: frida-tools/objection install failed. Fix the error above, then re-run.".into())
            .ok();
        return;
    }
    if !ssl_pin::push_frida_server(tx) {
        tx.send("Stopped: couldn't push/start frida-server. Fix the error above, then re-run.".into())
            .ok();
        return;
    }

    if !mitm::install(tx) {
        tx.send("Stopped: mitmproxy install failed. Fix the error above, then re-run.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    if !mitm::start_and_set_proxy(tx) {
        tx.send("Stopped: couldn't start mitmweb. Fix the error above, then re-run.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }
    if !mitm::install_cert(tx) {
        tx.send("Stopped: CA cert install failed. Fix the error above, then re-run.".into()).ok();
        return;
    }

    tx.send(String::new()).ok();
    tx.send("[OK] Sandbox ready: emulator booted, frida-server running, traffic".into()).ok();
    tx.send("routed through mitmweb with its CA trusted at the system level.".into()).ok();
    tx.send("Use 'SSL pinning bypass -> Bypass pinning for a package' per target app.".into()).ok();
}
```

`src/modules/update.rs`:

```rs
use crate::util::{cache, github, shell};

pub const REPO: &str = "0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit";
const ASSET_NAME: &str = "apkat.exe";

/// Checks GitHub's latest release against the running version, and if newer,
/// downloads it, swaps it in for the current exe, and relaunches — all before
/// the TUI starts. Silently gives up on any failure (offline, API down, no
/// release yet) so a normal launch never blocks on network issues.
pub fn check_and_apply(current_version: &str) {
    cleanup_previous_version();

    let Some((latest, asset_url)) = github::latest_release_asset(REPO, ASSET_NAME) else { return };
    if !github::is_newer(&latest, current_version) {
        return;
    }

    // A release whose uploaded exe doesn't actually report the new version
    // (e.g. Cargo.toml wasn't bumped before tagging) would otherwise redownload
    // the same broken asset forever, since every relaunch sees the same tag as
    // "newer". Once we've tried a given tag and it didn't move the version,
    // don't try it again until a genuinely different tag appears.
    let attempted_marker = cache::dir().join("update_attempted.tag");
    if std::fs::read_to_string(&attempted_marker).ok().as_deref() == Some(latest.as_str()) {
        return;
    }
    let _ = std::fs::write(&attempted_marker, &latest);

    println!("Update available: v{current_version} -> v{latest}. Downloading...");
    let Ok(current_exe) = std::env::current_exe() else { return };
    let new_path = current_exe.with_file_name("apkat.new.exe");
    let new_path_str = new_path.to_string_lossy().to_string();

    let (ok, out) = shell::run("curl", &["-L", "--max-time", "60", "-o", &new_path_str, &asset_url]);
    if !ok {
        eprintln!("Update download failed: {out}");
        return;
    }

    let old_path = current_exe.with_file_name("apkat.old.exe");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&old_path);
    if std::fs::rename(&current_exe, &old_path).is_err() {
        eprintln!("Update failed: couldn't move aside the running exe.");
        return;
    }
    if std::fs::rename(&new_path, &current_exe).is_err() {
        eprintln!("Update failed: couldn't install the new exe.");
        let _ = std::fs::rename(&old_path, &current_exe); // best-effort revert
        return;
    }

    println!("Updated to v{latest}, relaunching...");
    let _ = shell::spawn_bg(&current_exe.to_string_lossy(), &[]);
    std::process::exit(0);
}

/// Removes the previous version's renamed-aside exe, now that this (new)
/// process holds no lock on it — the one file Windows wouldn't let us delete
/// mid-update last launch.
fn cleanup_previous_version() {
    if let Ok(current_exe) = std::env::current_exe() {
        let old_path = current_exe.with_file_name("apkat.old.exe");
        let _ = std::fs::remove_file(old_path);
    }
}
```

```rs
use std::path::PathBuf;

/// Persistent download cache so re-running a step doesn't re-fetch what's
/// already on disk. Lives outside %TEMP% so it survives cleanup/reboots.
pub fn dir() -> PathBuf {
    let base = std::env::var("LOCALAPPDATA").or_else(|_| std::env::var("HOME")).unwrap_or_else(|_| ".".into());
    let path = PathBuf::from(base).join("apk-toolkit").join("cache");
    let _ = std::fs::create_dir_all(&path);
    path
}
```

`src/util/github.rs`:

```rs
use crate::util::shell;

/// Raw JSON body of a repo's `/releases/latest`. None on any failure
/// (offline, API down, no release yet).
pub fn fetch_release_json(repo: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let url = format!("https://api.github.com/repos/{repo}/releases/latest");
    let (ok, body) = shell::run("curl", &["-s", "--max-time", "8", "-H", "User-Agent: apk-toolkit", &url]);
    (ok && !body.is_empty()).then_some(body)
}

/// Returns (tag_name without leading 'v', asset download URL) for a repo's
/// latest release, picking the one asset whose name contains `asset_contains`.
/// Only safe when exactly one asset is expected to match — if a release has
/// several similarly-named assets (e.g. a GUI-only build alongside the full
/// one), fetch the JSON yourself and match on the exact, version-qualified name.
pub fn latest_release_asset(repo: &str, asset_contains: &str) -> Option<(String, String)> {
    let body = fetch_release_json(repo)?;
    let tag = json_string(&body, "tag_name")?;
    let version = tag.trim_start_matches('v').to_string();
    let asset_url = json_string_matching(&body, "browser_download_url", asset_contains)?;
    Some((version, asset_url))
}

/// First `"key": "value"` match.
pub fn json_string(json: &str, key: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let pat = format!("\"{key}\"");
    let after = &json[json.find(&pat)? + pat.len()..];
    let after = &after[after.find(':')? + 1..].trim_start();
    let after = after.strip_prefix('"')?;
    Some(after[..after.find('"')?].to_string())
}

/// First `"key": "value"` match whose value contains `must_contain` — used to
/// pick the right asset URL out of a release's (possibly multi-entry) asset list.
pub fn json_string_matching(json: &str, key: &str, must_contain: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let pat = format!("\"{key}\"");
    let mut search_from = 0;
    while let Some(rel_idx) = json[search_from..].find(&pat) {
        let idx = search_from + rel_idx;
        let after = &json[idx + pat.len()..];
        let Some(colon) = after.find(':') else { break };
        let after = after[colon + 1..].trim_start();
        if let Some(rest) = after.strip_prefix('"') {
            if let Some(end) = rest.find('"') {
                let value = &rest[..end];
                if value.contains(must_contain) {
                    return Some(value.to_string());
                }
                search_from = idx + pat.len() + colon + 1 + end;
                continue;
            }
        }
        break;
    }
    None
}

/// Plain numeric semver comparison (handles "0.9.0" < "0.10.0" correctly,
/// unlike a string compare).
pub fn is_newer(candidate: &str, current: &str) -> bool {
    let parse = |v: &str| -> Vec<u32> { v.split('.').map(|p| p.parse().unwrap_or(0)).collect() };
    parse(candidate) > parse(current)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    // Trimmed real-world shape: pretty-printed, multiple assets, non-matching
    // entries before the one we want (name differs from the URL's filename).
    const SAMPLE: &str = r#"{
  "url": "https://api.github.com/repos/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/releases/1",
  "tag_name": "v0.2.0",
  "name": "v0.2.0",
  "assets": [
    {
      "name": "apk-toolkit-source.tar.gz",
      "browser_download_url": "https://github.com/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/releases/download/v0.2.0/apk-toolkit-source.tar.gz"
    },
    {
      "name": "apk-toolkit.exe",
      "browser_download_url": "https://github.com/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/releases/download/v0.2.0/apk-toolkit.exe"
    }
  ]
}"#;

    #[test]
    fn parses_tag_and_picks_the_right_asset() {
        assert_eq!(json_string(SAMPLE, "tag_name").as_deref(), Some("v0.2.0"));
        assert_eq!(
            json_string_matching(SAMPLE, "browser_download_url", "apk-toolkit.exe").as_deref(),
            Some("https://github.com/0xramm/APK-Analysis-Toolkit/releases/download/v0.2.0/apk-toolkit.exe")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn version_compare_handles_double_digits() {
        assert!(is_newer("0.10.0", "0.9.0"));
        assert!(!is_newer("0.9.0", "0.10.0"));
        assert!(!is_newer("0.1.0", "0.1.0"));
    }
}
```

`src/util/sdk.rs`:

```rs
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

/// Finds the Android SDK root: env vars first, then common per-OS install locations.
pub fn sdk_root() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    for var in ["ANDROID_SDK_ROOT", "ANDROID_HOME"] {
        if let Ok(p) = std::env::var(var) {
            let p = PathBuf::from(p);
            if p.exists() {
                return Some(p);
            }
        }
    }

    let home = std::env::var("USERPROFILE").or_else(|_| std::env::var("HOME")).ok();
    let mut candidates = Vec::new();
    if let Ok(local) = std::env::var("LOCALAPPDATA") {
        candidates.push(PathBuf::from(local).join("Android").join("Sdk"));
    }
    if let Some(h) = &home {
        candidates.push(PathBuf::from(h).join("Library/Android/sdk"));
        candidates.push(PathBuf::from(h).join("Android/Sdk"));
    }
    candidates.into_iter().find(|p| p.exists())
}

pub fn emulator_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    let root = sdk_root()?;
    [root.join("emulator/emulator.exe"), root.join("emulator/emulator")]
        .into_iter()
        .find(|p| p.exists())
}

pub fn adb_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    let root = sdk_root()?;
    [root.join("platform-tools/adb.exe"), root.join("platform-tools/adb")]
        .into_iter()
        .find(|p| p.exists())
}

pub fn avdmanager_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    find_in_cmdline_tools(&sdk_root()?, "avdmanager")
}

pub fn sdkmanager_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    find_in_cmdline_tools(&sdk_root()?, "sdkmanager")
}

/// apksigner lives under build-tools/<version>/, picks the newest version present.
pub fn apksigner_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    build_tools_file(&["apksigner.bat", "apksigner"])
}

/// aapt (manifest/label/permissions dump) also lives under build-tools/<version>/.
pub fn aapt_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    build_tools_file(&["aapt.exe", "aapt"])
}

fn build_tools_file(names: &[&str]) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    let root = sdk_root()?;
    let base = root.join("build-tools");
    let mut versions: Vec<PathBuf> = std::fs::read_dir(&base).ok()?.flatten().map(|e| e.path()).collect();
    versions.sort(); // version dirs sort correctly as plain strings for this purpose
    versions.into_iter().rev().find_map(|dir| names.iter().map(|n| dir.join(n)).find(|p| p.exists()))
}

/// Git for Windows bundles xz/openssl/curl under its own bin dirs, which aren't
/// always on every shell's PATH even when they're on the "real" system PATH.
pub fn git_for_windows_tool(name: &str) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    for dir in [r"C:\Program Files\Git\mingw64\bin", r"C:\Program Files\Git\usr\bin"] {
        let candidate = Path::new(dir).join(format!("{name}.exe"));
        if candidate.exists() {
            return Some(candidate);
        }
    }
    None
}

/// cmdline-tools binaries live under cmdline-tools/<version>/bin/, version usually "latest".
fn find_in_cmdline_tools(root: &Path, tool: &str) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    let base = root.join("cmdline-tools");
    let mut dirs = vec![base.join("latest")];
    if let Ok(entries) = std::fs::read_dir(&base) {
        dirs.extend(entries.flatten().map(|e| e.path()));
    }
    for dir in dirs {
        for fname in [format!("{tool}.bat"), tool.to_string()] {
            let candidate = dir.join("bin").join(&fname);
            if candidate.exists() {
                return Some(candidate);
            }
        }
    }
    None
}
```

`src/util/shell.rs`:

```rs
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::process::{Command, Stdio};
use std::sync::mpsc::Sender;
use std::sync::{Mutex, OnceLock};

use super::sdk;

/// Windows can't exec .bat/.cmd directly via CreateProcess — route through cmd /C.
fn build(cmd: &str, args: &[&str]) -> Command {
    if cfg!(windows) && (cmd.ends_with(".bat") || cmd.ends_with(".cmd")) {
        let mut c = Command::new("cmd");
        c.arg("/C").arg(cmd).args(args);
        c
    } else {
        let mut c = Command::new(cmd);
        c.args(args);
        c
    }
}

/// Runs `cmd args...` and returns (success, combined stdout+stderr, trimmed).
/// stdin is nulled so a prompting subprocess can't hang the TUI thread.
pub fn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str]) -> (bool, String) {
    match build(cmd, args).stdin(Stdio::null()).output() {
        Ok(out) => {
            let mut s = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).to_string();
            s.push_str(&String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr));
            (out.status.success(), s.trim().to_string())
        }
        Err(e) => (false, format!("`{cmd}` not found: {e}")),
    }
}

/// Cheap "is this on PATH and does it respond" check.
pub fn is_installed(cmd: &str, version_arg: &str) -> bool {
    build(cmd, &[version_arg])
        .stdin(Stdio::null())
        .output()
        .map(|o| o.status.success())
        .unwrap_or(false)
}

/// Starts a long-running process and hands back the `Child` (e.g. so its
/// PID can be tracked and killed later, like a frida bypass session).
pub fn spawn_child(cmd: &str, args: &[&str]) -> Result<std::process::Child, String> {
    build(cmd, args)
        .stdin(Stdio::null())
        .stdout(Stdio::null())
        .stderr(Stdio::null())
        .spawn()
        .map_err(|e| format!("`{cmd}` failed to start: {e}"))
}

/// [`spawn_child`], discarding the handle, for fire-and-forget background processes.
pub fn spawn_bg(cmd: &str, args: &[&str]) -> Result<(), String> {
    spawn_child(cmd, args).map(|_| ())
}

/// Kills a process by PID (e.g. a tracked frida session).
pub fn kill_pid(pid: u32) -> (bool, String) {
    if cfg!(windows) {
        run("taskkill", &["/PID", &pid.to_string(), "/T", "/F"])
    } else {
        run("kill", &["-9", &pid.to_string()])
    }
}

/// Resolves a tool to something runnable: tries the bare name on PATH first,
/// then falls back to an SDK-discovered absolute path (Android Studio's tools
/// usually aren't on PATH even when installed).
pub fn locate(bare: &str, version_arg: &str, fallback: Option<PathBuf>) -> Option<String> {
    if is_installed(bare, version_arg) {
        return Some(bare.to_string());
    }
    let path = fallback?;
    let path_str = path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    is_installed(&path_str, version_arg).then_some(path_str)
}

/// `adb` resolved via PATH, falling back to the SDK-discovered path.
pub fn adb_bin() -> String {
    locate("adb", "version", sdk::adb_path()).unwrap_or_else(|| "adb".to_string())
}

/// (serial, state) for every device `adb devices` lists, e.g. `("emulator-5556", "device")`.
pub fn adb_devices() -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    let (ok, out) = run(&adb_bin(), &["devices"]);
    if !ok {
        return Vec::new();
    }
    out.lines()
        .skip(1)
        .filter_map(|l| {
            let mut parts = l.split_whitespace();
            Some((parts.next()?.to_string(), parts.next()?.to_string()))
        })
        .collect()
}

pub fn adb_device_connected() -> bool {
    adb_devices().iter().any(|(_, state)| state == "device")
}

fn selected_device_slot() -> &'static Mutex<Option<String>> {
    static SLOT: OnceLock<Mutex<Option<String>>> = OnceLock::new();
    SLOT.get_or_init(|| Mutex::new(None))
}

/// Manually pins which device adb actions target (set via the "Select ADB
/// device" menu). `None` goes back to auto-picking.
pub fn set_selected_device(serial: Option<String>) {
    *selected_device_slot().lock().unwrap() = serial;
}

pub fn selected_device() -> Option<String> {
    selected_device_slot().lock().unwrap().clone()
}

/// Picks which device `adb` commands should target: a manual selection if one
/// was made, else the first device actually online ("device"), else whatever's
/// listed (so an explicit -s still avoids adb's "more than one device" error),
/// else none.
pub fn adb_target() -> Option<String> {
    if let Some(serial) = selected_device() {
        return Some(serial);
    }
    let devices = adb_devices();
    devices
        .iter()
        .find(|(_, state)| state == "device")
        .or_else(|| devices.first())
        .map(|(serial, _)| serial.clone())
}

/// Runs `adb <args...>` targeted at the current device via -s, so it works
/// even when stale/ghost entries make a bare `adb` call ambiguous.
pub fn adb_run(args: &[&str]) -> (bool, String) {
    let bin = adb_bin();
    match adb_target() {
        Some(serial) => {
            let mut full: Vec<&str> = vec!["-s", &serial];
            full.extend_from_slice(args);
            run(&bin, &full)
        }
        None => run(&bin, args),
    }
}

/// Blocks until `serial` shows up in "device" state, or `timeout_secs` elapses.
/// `adb root`/`adb unroot` restart adbd, which disconnects and reconnects the
/// device — calling another adb command too soon can race that reconnect.
pub fn wait_for_device(serial: &str, timeout_secs: u64) -> bool {
    let start = std::time::Instant::now();
    while start.elapsed().as_secs() < timeout_secs {
        if adb_devices().iter().any(|(s, state)| s == serial && state == "device") {
            return true;
        }
        std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(400));
    }
    false
}

/// Background version of [`adb_run`] (e.g. `adb reboot`, backgrounded shell commands).
pub fn adb_spawn_bg(args: &[&str]) -> Result<(), String> {
    let bin = adb_bin();
    match adb_target() {
        Some(serial) => {
            let mut full: Vec<&str> = vec!["-s", &serial];
            full.extend_from_slice(args);
            spawn_bg(&bin, &full)
        }
        None => spawn_bg(&bin, args),
    }
}

/// Opens `url` in the default desktop browser.
pub fn open_url(url: &str) -> Result<(), String> {
    if cfg!(windows) {
        spawn_bg("cmd", &["/C", "start", "", url])
    } else if cfg!(target_os = "macos") {
        spawn_bg("open", &[url])
    } else {
        spawn_bg("xdg-open", &[url])
    }
}

/// Short, URL-safe local token — not a cryptographic secret, just enough to
/// keep mitmweb's UI from 403'ing on the auto-opened tab.
pub fn random_token() -> String {
    use std::collections::hash_map::DefaultHasher;
    use std::hash::{Hash, Hasher};
    let nanos = std::time::SystemTime::now()
        .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
        .map(|d| d.as_nanos())
        .unwrap_or(0);
    let mut hasher = DefaultHasher::new();
    nanos.hash(&mut hasher);
    std::process::id().hash(&mut hasher);
    format!("{:016x}", hasher.finish())
}

/// Runs `cmd args...`, forwarding its output to `tx` live instead of only at
/// the end — needed for long downloads/installs (sdkmanager, pip, curl) so
/// the TUI doesn't sit silent for minutes and look frozen. Progress bars that
/// update in place via `\r` (not `\n`) are forwarded as lines prefixed with
/// `\r`, which the TUI's log treats as "replace the last line" instead of
/// appending — so a real percentage-style progress indicator shows up live.
pub fn run_streaming(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], tx: &Sender<String>) -> (bool, String) {
    let mut child = match build(cmd, args).stdin(Stdio::null()).stdout(Stdio::piped()).stderr(Stdio::piped()).spawn() {
        Ok(c) => c,
        Err(e) => return (false, format!("`{cmd}` not found: {e}")),
    };

    let captured = std::sync::Arc::new(Mutex::new(String::new()));

    let stderr_handle = child.stderr.take().map(|s| {
        let tx = tx.clone();
        let captured = std::sync::Arc::clone(&captured);
        std::thread::spawn(move || read_progress_stream(s, &tx, &captured))
    });
    if let Some(stdout) = child.stdout.take() {
        read_progress_stream(stdout, tx, &captured);
    }
    if let Some(h) = stderr_handle {
        let _ = h.join();
    }

    match child.wait() {
        Ok(status) => (status.success(), captured.lock().unwrap().trim().to_string()),
        Err(e) => (false, format!("`{cmd}` wait failed: {e}")),
    }
}

/// Reads raw bytes (not `BufRead::lines`, which only splits on `\n` and would
/// swallow `\r`-updated progress bars entirely) and forwards each completed
/// line, prefixing `\r`-terminated ones so the TUI knows to overwrite in place.
fn read_progress_stream(mut r: impl std::io::Read, tx: &Sender<String>, captured: &std::sync::Mutex<String>) {
    let mut buf = [0u8; 4096];
    let mut line: Vec<u8> = Vec::new();
    loop {
        let n = match r.read(&mut buf) {
            Ok(0) | Err(_) => break,
            Ok(n) => n,
        };
        for &b in &buf[..n] {
            if b == b'\n' || b == b'\r' {
                let text = String::from_utf8_lossy(&line).trim().to_string();
                if !text.is_empty() {
                    let mut c = captured.lock().unwrap();
                    c.push_str(&text);
                    c.push('\n');
                    drop(c);
                    let overwrite = b == b'\r';
                    tx.send(if overwrite { format!("\r{text}") } else { text }).ok();
                }
                line.clear();
            } else {
                line.push(b);
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Copies `text` to the OS clipboard via the platform's built-in tool
/// (`clip` on Windows, `pbcopy` on macOS, `xclip` elsewhere) — no crate needed.
pub fn copy_to_clipboard(text: &str) -> Result<(), String> {
    use std::io::Write;

    let (cmd, args): (&str, &[&str]) = if cfg!(windows) {
        ("clip", &[])
    } else if cfg!(target_os = "macos") {
        ("pbcopy", &[])
    } else {
        ("xclip", &["-selection", "clipboard"])
    };

    let mut child = build(cmd, args)
        .stdin(Stdio::piped())
        .stdout(Stdio::null())
        .stderr(Stdio::null())
        .spawn()
        .map_err(|e| format!("`{cmd}` not available: {e}"))?;
    child
        .stdin
        .take()
        .ok_or("no stdin handle")?
        .write_all(text.as_bytes())
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    child.wait().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    Ok(())
}

```
